# wujiang_game Project Map

- 2026-11-03 BP特殊规则源表新增“攻+1”（所有单位攻+1），并修订“真回合制”为禁普通移动及位移技能、范覆盖全图。`rooms/special_modes.py` 为名单/展示源，`BPSpecialModeEffect` 持有战斗限制和全体加攻；范围最终覆盖通过 `BattleFieldEffect.adjust_unit_targeting_range` 窄接口，保留“同情弱者”范1优先。规则文档和行为场景同步，动态验收待用户统一执行。

- 2026-11-03 自走棋金币结算：基础收入每轮+7，胜利+1，连胜第3场起+1/+2/+3/+4封顶，连败第3场起+1/+2封顶，利息每10存款+1最多3；取消扣对手血量对应的金币。点金手等装备专属产金保留。`rooms/autochess.py` 为结算归属，界面/文档/行为场景同步。文档仅用本项目名称，不引用外部游戏品牌；动态测试待用户统一执行。

- 2026-11-03 自走棋商店抽将：五格抽到已有武将的概率固定为20/15/10/5/1%，与玩家等级无关；普通分支从未拥有武将按玩家等级五档权重有放回抽样。用户选择每次商店生成/刷新使用独立新种子，保存该次种子；不做档内轮换，也不保证不同玩家结果接近。实现、展示分别在 `rooms/autochess.py` 与 `static/tactical/autochess-ui.js`；规则见 `docs/自走棋模式规则.md`，动态体验待用户统一验证。

- 2026-11-03 后续武将维护必须先读现行 OOP 所有权与调用链，将战斗规则放在技能/特性对象、AI 差异放在对象钩子、跨武将共性放在窄共享服务；旧中央分支不能作为新代码模板。近期源表同步入口 `heroes/excel_roster_data.py`，机制在 `heroes/mechanics/`，BP 在 `rooms/special_battle.py`，设计档案在 JSON 与问题清单的设计思想页，预测证书改动后需同步来源指纹及契约。此次新批静态检查，动态待用户统一运行。

- 2026-11-03 用户统一验收：`reports/all-hero-deep-audit/current/` 的内嵌完整回归 2761 项全部通过、9 跳过；125 将/1250 场审计 0 高信号、0 崩溃。22 场均因 500 步上限无胜负，其余 9012 条均为信息。此前检查点崩溃修复已通过本次完整审计。

- 2026-11-03 全审计高信号收敛：159条R373超频、2条R070天罚、1条R257扫射的空载荷均由对象策略明确过滤无正收益或无可封印技能，审计记录 `expected_filter_reason`，不再误报生成缺口。鬼步后续改变震开路径时，震开单个目标路径落空不终止整链。原生崩溃栈在 `DirectSnapshot.restore` 的命名空间恢复，改用 Python 对象赋值，槽与容器仍保留原生恢复；待用户全量重跑确认。

- 2026-11-03 审计归因：旧报告30条动作错误中，已确认19条是不可见敌人占格。AI继续遵守视野，审计只将核实碰撞记为信息。连锁已付费落空、超频旧区域、浮游炮前移禁攻、战斧费用等共通问题已修并跑短局；原生崩溃与全量审计仍待复核。

- 2026-11-02 第二次统一回归：真实浏览器自登录门至战场全部通过。完整 unittest 2598 项中 1 失败、2 导入错误、8 跳过；两个预测测试文件缺独立 `src` 路径已修，战场恢复首次状态读取误推进到期 AI 动作已改为只呈现检查点（恢复定向 3/3 通过），地狱再临特性 OOP 属性令预测因子化证书拒绝已按原始受审继承和安全回退修正（预测 112/112、直接预期 46/46 通过）。长套件需用户复跑；全武将审计的既有 30 条动作错误与 1 场原生工作进程崩溃仍需独立归因。

- 2026-11-02 统一验证反馈：前端模块命令因 `node` 未在 PowerShell PATH 中而未运行；用本机缓存 `node.exe` 的绝对路径运行返回 `LINK OK`。浏览器启动检查报“服务未能启动”，根因是 `RoomLobbyMixin.auto_configure` 默认参数引用原模块常量，类加载触发 `NameError`。两个默认值已放入独立 `room_defaults.py`，服务导入、`GameRoom` 创建及默认值短检查通过；启动检查现显示服务退出码与错误尾部。审计回归日志另有 8 个倾向 AI 轻量对象缺 `get_skill` 的错误，兼容查询已修复且定向 21/21 通过；审计 1250 场有 30 条行动执行错误及 1 场工作进程崩溃，须单独归因。真实浏览器重跑和完整动态验收待用户结果。

- 2026-11-02 主结构迁移完成：`Battle` 的动作/反应/结算/伤害/移动/回合/单位/生命周期/视图拆成 11 个职责 mixin，`core.py` 约 7897→3134 行；`GameRoom` 的 BP/教程/模拟/大厅/回合/视图/对局拆成 7 个 mixin，`multiplayer.py` 4610→1601 行。共享 AI 的影响、R12/R13/R15、两种嘲讽和红色装备准备评分继续归技能/策略对象，预测继承链与契约同步。按本轮主结构范围代码迁移 100%，战场 ≥1000/2000/5000 行文件 9/2/0；动态验收未做，不能称行为已通过。用户统一测试后按结果修复。详见 OOP 计划。

- 2026-11-01 共享 AI 延伸：有效敌方影响、单体控制效果和召唤空位例外继续归 `Skill` 钩子或类声明，`ai_impact_scoring.py` 固定代码条件进一步减少；预测源指纹/契约同步。用户要求本批不运行任何测试，全部行为验证待统一执行。整体结构粗估约 85%，≥1000/2000/5000 行战场文件仍为 9/3/1；`core.py` 7897 行、`multiplayer.py` 4610 行仍待职责拆分。详见 OOP 计划。

- 2026-11-01 技能评分中央分支整段迁移：`rooms/ai_skill_dispatch_scoring.py` 571→137 行，顶层武将/技能代码条件 52→6，余下六处仅通用技能类别。武将专属评分分别由技能 `ai_priority_score`、`ai_move_score`、`ai_heal_score` 与 `HeroAIPolicy` 负责；共享服务只保留命名估值和试算。六组固定见证 170 场景/396 评分、35 将 96 场景/1208 候选全部全等，408 项定向测试通过，预测契约已更新。整体约70%，大文件 11/3/1；全库和全公开审计待 AI 段稳定后用户重跑。下一步归因独立审计动作错误，整理两个千行共享评分文件，再进入引擎/多人房间。详见 OOP 计划。

- 2026-11-01 用户长测回归：完整 unittest 2748 项有 2 失败/9 跳过，已修复迁移后测试补丁目标；1250 场审计中 982 条重复决策/行动错误归于 R320 渔夫直接评分掷币和必选提示回退，原种子现 39 步结束。龙区域普攻假设机会的 13 条候选构造错误改由 `AIAttackScoring.legal_attack_payload` 从预览选区域，三个原种子短局无此错。最终 180 项定向测试及 35 将 1208 候选全等；其余 30 次独立行动错误待查。整体约 60–65%，大型文件 11/3/1；下一步消除技能分派对象 52 个顶层技能代码条件，再处理引擎/多人房间。详见 OOP 计划。

- 2026-10-31 中央 AI 职责拆分：`rooms/ai.py` 7440→798 行，阵型/攻击/技能/反应/效果/支援等共享评分及查询迁入注入服务的 `ai_*_scoring.py` 对象；分类常量和类型移入 `ai_skill_catalog.py`、`ai_types.py`，共享适配器移入 `ai_room_services.py`。预测自恢复函数与部分安全认证入口留在兼容层；预测契约重新固定。575 项定向/共享测试通过，35 将 1208 候选及 16 将 25 步短局/147 发现与基线一致。整体约 60–65%，大文件 11/3/1；技能分派服务仍有 52 个技能代码条件，下一步按 `Skill`/`HeroAIPolicy` 归属，不可将搬迁视为多态完成。详见 OOP 计划。

- 2026-10-19 用户整批缓存复跑：suite81项、4×26单位48步均等价；四场计算13.839→7.671秒、最慢1.981→0.852秒、一圈2.313→1.599秒。本批`ai_acceleration.py`给三类伤害探针加纯结果缓存（`ai.py`原探针体不改）：保完整局面/RNG/两ID/全部参数及跨参数—战场可变别名；同Pickler连续dump战场和参数，保memo链接，不能分开pickle后只比内容。外部技能/对象参数、副作用/编号消耗/非有限结果继续原计算；命中返回新DamageImpact。低命中决策自动停用该探针缓存，仍算全部候选；成功/拒绝条目纳入预算。新增7项，现44项，仅静态检查，动态收益待四场及原套件复跑。

Update this file after every new gameplay requirement or code change in `wujiang_game`. Keep entries concise and durable; do not paste full docs.

- 2026-10-19 BP 默认与名称：新建房间默认 `tier`，界面称“标准模式（等级随机规则）”；`full` 称“沙盒模式（全局选择规则）”。服务端公开名、建房设置及大厅标题/说明同步；内部 `tier`/`full` 值和旧中文输入别名保留，旧存档缺字段仍解释为旧版 `full`。场景见 `BPRoomBehaviorTests`，动态测试按约定延后。

- 2026-10-18 用户授权完整AI精确缓存与CPU并行：v2套件81项全部相等，决策总工作负载中位合计减少8.2%，区域批处理37.3倍；仍有区域射程和共享位置收益热点。`engine/geometry.py`有界缓存整数几何，`Battle.cells_within_distance`供矩形/区域中心复用；`rooms/ai_acceleration.py`按序列化战斗+RNG+绑定信息做决策内共享位置收益缓存，仅留战场/RNG/两类ID均不变的有限标量；>=12单位时可用共享2进程隔离预计算，加载不一致/副作用/失败全部原串行回退。`platform/id_sequence.py`保持原编号序列并提供无消耗查看；`run.py`避免spawn入口导入服务器副作用。v3对照增加ID位置与缓存/进程指标，`--workers 2/0`分别量总优化与缓存串行；原v2报告保留。新增行为场景仅静态检查，动态等价和实际多核收益待用户运行，详见`docs/完整AI等价优化.md`。

- 2026-10-18 完整AI等价提速续做：用户首批6项通过、四个对照相等，单次耗时约降7%～18%；读取报告确认都是吟游诗人当前回合，不能推广复杂场景。`rooms/ai.py::_AreaRecipientIndex`在去重/排序的各只读批次复用身体格索引，保受体顺序、真实命中数和原截取规则，不跨探针/动作。对照工具以`tee`还原声明和召唤单位两种ID，移除弃用警告；`--suite --profile`轮转全阵容、多单位/召唤物样本、独立48单位576候选批处理、交替重复中位数与另跑调用分析，v2默认报告不覆盖首批证据。新增4项行为场景（累计10项）；本轮仅静态检查，新增动态等价与性能待用户运行。细节见`docs/完整AI等价优化.md`。

- 2026-09-18 R10 Natsume completed: 36/73 inspected-and-modified, 37 remaining; 38 behavior scenarios, static checks only. R10 totals 109 scenarios. Friendly basic support includes self, grants actual mana and one persistent shared mark without damage/shield loss, with matching preview/summary. Wind Word only heals then attempts a straight full-body pull from frozen targeting; no arbitrary neighboring fallback. Wind Wall compares actual affected recipients and preventable outcomes, includes remote marked allies, piercing and pure control, keeps protection for the whole action and expires on its resolution token even without a chain. Successful application consumes the mark. Dispel freezes 11x11 cells, applies both-side defenses and recipient dedup, returns mana only for real destruction/reveal, preserves unrelated statuses and hides target identities in previews. Skill.target_can_react_to_effect skips unaffected Dispel recipients. AI scores formal support, healing/pull risk, actual purge net value, control protection and reachable Shensu gains while preserving last wall mana; probes and clone disguise remain intact. Next R11: Aaron, Lao Mage, Sakura Fire; lock all three source-only baselines before their code. Consolidated dynamic validation remains pending.

- 2026-09-17 R10 Fusion Man completed: 35/73 inspected-and-modified, 38 remaining; 36 behavior scenarios, static checks only. Nuclear Rush freezes eight-direction front-four cells and fifth-cell landing, preserves clipped/immobile attacks, attempts a legal full-body landing from the actual current position without normal movement cost, and never moves after cancellation/death. Circle attack hits each enemy recipient once. Shared destruction capture stores per-event count and final real footprint before removal, with reentry protection; Fusion explosion uses point attack power, half-pierce, both-side recipient dedup, and can trigger after revival but not living removal/banish/clones/summons. Real movement updates temporary declared-origin tracking, including death at the landing. Common Pierce deduplicates mounted recipients. AI compares stance/boost reachable outcomes and landing exposure, prices chained friendly explosion losses, conserves evasion mana, and rolls probes back fully. Natsume is next; its R10 source baseline is already locked, full battle/AI/test review remains pending.

- Audit evidence entry (2026-09-06): `src/wujiang/tools/audit_review_packet.py` indexes every saved match trace, all findings categories (including ordinary observations), every unittest FAIL/ERROR header, per-hero coverage notes, and separate staged/unstaged Git patches plus untracked paths. `detail_test_all_wujiangs.py --current` writes `review_packet.md/json` after the unchanged full regression + current-batch matches. `--current --summarize-only` reads existing evidence without tests/simulation; it cannot accept current code. Git changes are relative to HEAD/index, not prior acceptance; ignored and generated outputs/reports are excluded from untracked enumeration. Keep the locked baseline and inspect all findings, changed shared contracts, gaps, and representative decisions; the compact index is not automatic design verification.

- Area basic-attack target collection is a shared contract across declaration and resolution: combine payload-level friendly fire (for siege attacks), component-level ally effects (for hero area attacks), a forced allied basic-attack target, and siege structure direct-hit filtering. Merge work in `tactical/engine/core.py` must preserve all four instead of selecting one branch's target filter.

The codebase was reorganised by domain on 2026-08-27. Every phase record further down still cites the pre-reorganisation paths (`web/server.py`, `strategy/*.py`, `engine/core.py`, `static/app.js`). Those records describe what was true when they were written and are deliberately left unchanged; use the map below to translate them.

- `src/wujiang/platform/` — HTTP kernel (`http/handler.py` dispatch, `http/runtime.py` shared stores, `http/routing.py` route registry, `http/server.py` composition root), plus `auth.py`, `analytics.py`, `match_history.py`, `observability.py`, `rate_limit.py`, `security.py`, `deployment.py`. Contains no gameplay knowledge and must never import `tactical` or `strategic`.
- `src/wujiang/tactical/` — `engine/` (was `engine/`), `heroes/` (was `heroes/`), `rooms/` (was `web/multiplayer.py`, `ai.py`, `replay.py`, `postgame.py`, `tutorial.py`, `onboarding.py`, plus new `rooms/history.py`), `session.py`, and `api.py` for the 34 battle routes.
- `src/wujiang/strategic/` — everything that was `strategy/`, plus `campaign_runtime.py` (campaign store singleton and shared helpers), `service.py` (order validation, command accounting, action queue; was inline in `web/server.py`) and `api.py` for the 31 campaign routes.
- `src/wujiang/bridge/battle_bridge.py` — the only channel between the two game domains: campaign conflict to grid room, and battle result back to campaign state. `tactical` and `strategic` must not import each other.
- `static/` mirrors the split as native ES modules: `core/`, `platform/`, `tactical/`, `strategic/`, and `bridge/campaign-battle.js`. `app.js` is now a 34-line entry point. `home-ui.js`, `replay-ui.js` and `battle-feedback.js` moved under `platform/` and `tactical/` but remain classic scripts using their `globalThis.Wujiang*` namespaces, because the quickjs behaviour tests evaluate them directly.
- Routes are registered per domain with `@get` / `@post` from `wujiang.platform.http.routing`; the kernel owns dispatch only, and `platform/http/server.py` assembles the app by importing the three `api` modules. Readiness dependencies register themselves via `register_dependency`.
- Frontend assertions now read the whole of `static/` through `frontend_source()` / `frontend_module()` in `tests/test_behavior.py`, so moving code between modules is no longer a test failure. `tests/test_frontend_modules.py` plus `tools/verify_frontend_modules.mjs` link-check the real module graph and enforce that the two frontend domains stay independent.
- Screens are a first-class concept in `static/core/router.js`: `gate`, `menu`, `draft`, `battle`, one visible at a time, each backed by `#<name>-screen`. Login is a hard gate — `render()` short-circuits to the gate screen when logged out, matching the backend, which already 401s every gameplay POST. The main menu deliberately has no single/multiplayer split: every session has slots, AI holds the empty ones, and humans can take over, join late or spectate.
- The address bar names the destination, never the container. `draft` hosts four unrelated flows (`campaign`, `skirmish`, `tutorial`, `archive`), so a `#draft` hash says nothing about where the player is — and the old `screenHash()` wrote it for every non-battle screen including the menu, so the next visit skipped the menu and dropped into whichever flow was default. Flow names double as hashes; `routeTarget()` sends anything unrecognised to the menu.
- Campaign and skirmish sit side by side in the menu. The axis is whether you run a map, not how many humans play: skirmish creates rooms and invite codes too. Its three entries used to be split across two unrelated flows, so nobody could tell they led to the same thing.
- Copy belongs to the markup. `hydrateStaticLabels()` used to rewrite twenty-odd labels at boot that `index.html` already stated, so new button text would silently revert to the old wording.
- `static/theme.css` holds the design tokens, base reset and component layer (`.btn`, `.input`, `.field`, `.panel`, `.tabs`, `.chip`, `.hint`) and must load before `styles.css`, whose tail aliases the legacy token names onto the dark ones. `static/core/components.js` is the matching JS factory, since the UI is built imperatively and CSS classes alone do not enforce consistency.
- A module must not write shared chrome unless it owns the current screen; `renderRoomPanels()` used to overwrite the page header even while the player was in a campaign. The topbar now belongs to `static/core/topbar.js`; it used to be `renderNavigation()` in the tactical domain.
- Show nothing until the destination screen is known. Some screen has to be visible in the markup and it is not necessarily the one the player lands on, so the page flashed the room lobby before jumping to the menu. `body.is-booting` keeps the shell `visibility: hidden` until `applyScreen()` runs — and the failure path must clear it too, or the error banner hides with it.
- A room is something you are looking at, not a standing identity. The lobby shows whenever a room is in hand regardless of flow, so a stale `?room=` in the URL covers whatever you picked from the menu — picking campaign lands you in a skirmish room. `leaveRoomView()` (`tactical/session.js`) drops the room from view on every menu departure; the server-side room and the stored seat identity survive, reachable via `#room-directory` on the skirmish screen or the invite link.
- Side-by-side buttons cannot express priority. `createMenu()` collapses secondary actions so the remaining one carries weight; `createMenu({ children })` adopts existing buttons, keeping their IDs, listeners and `.hidden` toggles. Two traps: `[hidden]`'s `display: none` comes from the UA sheet and loses to an author `display: flex`, so `.menu__list[hidden]` must restate it or the popover never closes; and adopted buttons carry `.ghost`/`.btn` borders, so `.menu__item` needs the `.menu__list` prefix to win.
- Do not name lookup-able nodes through `createMenu`'s `id` option — `verify_frontend_dom_ids.py` reads `.id = "..."` assignments, and treating every `id:` option literal as a known DOM ID would gut the check. Assign `node.id` explicitly.
- Put a hint's `id` on `.hint__bubble`, not the outer `.hint`: existing code writes `note.textContent`, which would erase the icon and structure.
- Prose earns its place only if it can change the reader's next move. Two failure modes were everywhere in the campaign screens and are worth recognising by shape. **Always-true captions**: "邀请已锁定 · 当前账号可直接恢复" printed on every card, or a three-line status strip whose three lines all mean "this campaign has started" — text that is identical in every state carries no information. **Restating the widget**: "点击城市选择命令目标", "选择一个战役后会显示城市、科技和事件", "行动队列为空" next to a visibly empty queue. Rules and read-once explanations belong in a `createHint()` bubble; per-item facts stay inline; sections with nothing to report should not render their heading at all (see `renderStrategyRecoveryOverview`, which used to draw a title plus "可恢复 0 场…" plus "还没有检查点"). Same for permanently-disabled buttons — "推进一月" on the campaign list has no month to advance, so it hides rather than sitting greyed out.
- `tools/verify_frontend_boot.py` drives Chrome over CDP (`tools/cdp.py`, standard library only) through gate, menu, campaign list, live campaign, skirmish and battle, and asserts each screen's hash. Walk all the way through: each screen is reachable only via the one before it, so a screen no check opens is a screen that breaks unnoticed. The live campaign is its own step because it shares almost no code with the list that leads to it -- assigning to an SVG's read-only `className` crashed the whole map while the list still looked fine, so every check passed and the screen behind the button rendered nothing. That step also asserts the campaign screen does not exceed the viewport. Snapshot flags like `--dump-dom` fire before the app finishes awaiting `/api/auth/me`, so they always capture the login gate.
- Campaigns can be deleted, not just archived. Archiving means "this run is over, keep it for the retrospective"; archived campaigns stay in the list, so with only archive the list could never be cleaned up. `delete_campaign` is owner-only and deletes one `strategy_campaigns` row — every child table declares `ON DELETE CASCADE` and the connection sets `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, so they follow. The audit log is deliberately exempt: it is a hash-chained append-only log with no foreign key on `campaign_id`, and removing entries would break the chain. Write the audit row *before* the delete — `_audit_operation` looks the actor's username up in `strategy_members`, which is about to be cascaded away.
- A button whose only effect is invisible reads as broken. "离线" always called `mark_offline` correctly, but the campaign card never showed presence, so clicking it changed nothing on screen. It now appears only while you are actually online and disappears once clicked.
- `core/dialog.js` `confirmDialog()` returns `Promise<boolean>` and replaces `window.confirm` for destructive actions (delete campaign, delete room, leave room, surrender, log out). Native confirms look nothing like the app, cannot reword their buttons, and block the page. Focus defaults to Cancel; the `keydown` listener is on the capture phase so global shortcuts (space to pause, arrows to pick cells) do not fire while it is open.
- The account menu holds exactly three items: profile, back to main menu, log out. "Room lobby" used to be there too but its visibility condition was identical to the contextual primary button beside it, and "copy invite" already lives in the room header overflow. Dropdown item heights must be set by `.menu__list .menu__item` (fixed `min-height` + `line-height: 1`), never inherited: adopted buttons carry `.btn` / `.primary` / `.ghost` padding and end up uneven.
- Campaign screen shape lives in `static/strategic/campaign-shell.js`: hero rail left, map centre, inspector right, month bar bottom. `body.campaign-mode` (the one-viewport lock) applies only once `status === "active"`; during lobby the screen still stacks the origin-choice panel, lock banner and lock button above the war room, and locking the height would subtract all of that from the map. In lobby the three columns instead get a height band plus `grid-template-rows: minmax(0, 1fr)` -- max-height alone does not work, because an `auto` row track sizes to the tallest column's content and simply overflows the capped container. Two more traps. Buttons cannot be *moved* into the turn bar -- `#strategy-current` is cleared with `innerHTML = ""` on every render, which would delete them; the bar creates its own and forwards clicks, copying the disabled state so the judgement stays in one place. And locking the screen to one viewport needs the whole chain flexible: `.shell` at `height: 100vh` plus `display: flex` + `flex: 1 1 auto` + `min-height: 0` on `#draft-screen`, `.draft`, `#room-home` and `.strategy-panel`. Missing `display: flex` is the subtle one -- the parent offers a height but a plain block child never looks at it.
- `tools/verify_frontend_dom_ids.py` catches `$("...")` lookups whose element was deleted from the markup — invisible to every other static check, and it surfaces at runtime as a caught `null` error that freezes a button with a clean console.
- Colour literals do not follow the token remap. When something still looks light, it is hardcoded: sweep it into a token rather than patching the one rule.
- No gradient backgrounds on chrome; gradients are reserved for game surface and signal (pieces, mana pips, battle VFX, colourblind hatching, the strategy map). Nested frames are flattened too: `#room-home > *` strips borders from the flow panels because the outer `.draft.panel` already provides the panel feel. The id selector is deliberate — the panels' own border declarations sit later in `styles.css` and win at equal specificity.
- Full regression after the reorganisation is 767 passing with 26 environment skips. Run tests from the repository root; `replays/` is resolved relative to the working directory.
- Full layering rules and rationale: `docs/架构分层说明.md`.

- Phase 7 P7.1 is implemented in `strategy/store.py`, `web/server.py`, `strategy/ai.py` and the war-room UI. Real initial members may enter, resume, inspect and issue authorized orders asynchronously without every human being online. Each human has one persisted per-month `drafting`, `ready`, or `proxy_ai` state. Ready/proxied members cannot change orders until they withdraw. The host is implicitly readied when requesting advance; other humans must be ready or proxied. A ready host may close the month only for currently offline drafting humans, enabling same-rule AI for those factions after already queued actions and only with remaining command. Online humans cannot be forcibly proxied; membership, faction and office identity do not change, and every new month starts drafting. Full regression is 699 passing with 8 environment skips.
- Phase 7 P7.2 is implemented in `strategy/store.py`, `heroes.py`, `offices.py`, `web/server.py` and the war-room UI. The fixed two-major campaign supports 2–4 humans with at most two per faction; default joins balance factions while an explicit join-host-faction option creates co-op. Every human retains one unique controlled hero and formal office; the first faction member is Lord and the second starts as capital Governor. Confirmed handover swaps hero offices, while a Lord-requested non-Lord vacate requires the holder's acceptance. The current human Lord may grant one vacant non-Lord office to the other same-faction drafting human for the current month; the temporary holder uses the ordinary office permission resolver, cannot be self/cross-faction/ready/proxied, and cannot relinquish while its signed orders remain pending. Grant, revoke and expiry are persisted and public to the faction; settlement resolves the month's orders before expiring the takeover back to vacant. Pending actions or ready/proxy status block formal changes, requests expire across months, and campaign-host settlement authority remains independent of the in-world Lord office. - Phase 7 P7.3.1 invitation and account recovery is implemented in `strategy/store.py`, `web/server.py` and the strategy UI. Invitations persist as `open`, `revoked`, or `locked`; only the lobby host may revoke the current code or reissue a different code, revoked/old codes fail immediately, and a locked campaign cannot reopen invitations. Join codes are only new-member lobby credentials. Existing members recover campaign lists and enter with a fresh login session through account-bound membership, without the old code or a room token. Campaign cards, member panels and status strips explain that distinction. Full regression is 702 passing with 8 environment skips; P7.3.2 strategic battle checkpoint/restart recovery is next.
- Phase 7 P7.3 strategic battle recovery is implemented in `strategy/store.py`, `web/multiplayer.py`, `web/server.py`, `heroes/excel_roster.py` and the strategy UI. Every real strategic grid room persists a versioned, hashed authoritative checkpoint bound to its campaign, `PendingBattle`, original account participants and room id. Room reads/actions/AI/finish refresh it; a fresh login after process-memory loss recovers the same room, seat token and exact battle state. Corrupt checkpoints return an explicit restart-required state and only original participants may explicitly rebuild the same room from the immutable prebattle snapshot, without redeclaring the attack, paying costs, duplicating a pending battle or writing a result. Campaign payloads and the war room now expose a centralized per-battle recovery overview before room entry. Archive transitions persist at campaign level; original members can recover maps, retrospective and their own historical battles after another restart, while campaign/world orders and all strategic-room mutation endpoints enforce read-only state. The full interruption walkthrough covers checkpoint, process-memory loss, fresh login, exact resume, idempotent result writeback, archive, second restart and mutation rejection. Full regression is 705 passing with 8 environment skips. P7.4 migrations/backups/audit/admin repair is next.
- Phase 7 P7.4.1 versioned migration is implemented in `strategy/migrations.py`, `strategy/errors.py`, `strategy/models.py` and `strategy/store.py`. Every canonical `WorldState` writes save format version 1; missing-version legacy payloads are version 0 and migrate through a pure ordered pipeline. Strategy schema versions are module-private rather than shared SQLite `user_version`; the transactional applied-step ledger now reaches version 3 after the P7.4.3 audit-table migration. A successful save migration atomically records campaign/from/to/time, full pre-migration JSON and before/after SHA-256 hashes; repeated service starts do not rerun it. Invalid, negative or future save/schema versions fail with a conflict and remain untouched. Strategy tests are 179 passing; full regression is 708 passing with 8 environment skips. P7.4.2 automatic backup/retention/recovery drill is next.
- Phase 7 P7.4.2 backup/recovery drill is implemented in `strategy/backup.py`, `strategy/store.py`, `src/wujiang/tools/strategy_backup.py` and `tools/strategy_backup.py`. SQLite's online backup API snapshots the whole shared database before schema migration and before the first legacy world migration. Each managed snapshot has a versioned manifest with reason, automatic/manual category, time, size, SHA-256 and strategy versions; mismatches fail closed. The default retention keeps 12 valid automatic snapshots while never pruning manual backups. A drill restores only into an isolated temporary database, then checks SQLite integrity, foreign keys, every campaign world/version and every strategic battle checkpoint hash before deleting the drill copy; it never modifies the live DB. - Phase 7 P7.4.4 restricted repair is implemented in `strategy/repair.py`, `strategy/store.py`, `src/wujiang/tools/strategy_repair.py`, `tools/strategy_repair.py` and `docs/战略数据故障处理手册.md`. Apply requires an external 16+ character approval secret, traceable operator, reason code and exact plan id; changed/tampered/empty/blocked plans fail closed. Every committed repair/restore is sanitized and appended to the tamper-evident audit chain. - Phase 7 P7.5.1 request/transport security is implemented in `web/security.py`, `web/server.py`, `run.py` and `static/app.js`. Development/test/production profiles fail on invalid security configuration; production defaults to HTTPS-only and disables URL query auth. Forwarded scheme/host are honored only from configured proxy CIDRs. Before dispatch, POST requires allowed exact Origin when present, application/json object bodies and a configurable 256 KiB default limit. All responses receive CSP/frame/MIME/referrer/permissions/cross-origin headers, API responses are no-store, production HTTPS adds HSTS, and the Server header hides Python. Frontend session restoration now uses Bearer auth without copying tokens into URLs. Security tests are 4 passing, behavior tests 178 passing, and full regression is 728 passing with 8 environment skips. P7.5.2 session hardening/tiered rate limiting is next.
- Phase 7 P7.5.2 authentication/session hardening and tiered rate limiting is implemented in `web/auth.py`, `web/rate_limit.py`, `web/security.py` and `web/server.py`. New passwords require 8 characters and use PBKDF2-SHA256 at 300k iterations; successful login upgrades older hashes while legacy 6–7 character accounts remain valid and unknown-account verification uses equal hash work. New sessions store only SHA-256 token digests; existing plaintext session rows are scrubbed in place without invalidating clients. Sessions have 30-day absolute and 7-day idle expiry, five-minute touch throttling and an eight-active-session account cap. A bounded thread-safe sliding window separately protects auth IP/hashed identity, campaign join, analytics and mutations; join/mutation tiers check trusted effective client IP plus token digest, return 429, Retry-After and RateLimit headers, and never retain raw credentials. Auth tests are 8 passing, web-security tests 6 passing, behavior tests 178 passing, and full regression is 735 passing with 8 environment skips. P7.5.3 observability is next.
- Phase 7 P7.5.3 observability is implemented in `web/observability.py`, the four SQLite stores, `web/server.py` and `docs/上线监控与告警手册.md`. Public liveness is process-only; readiness initializes and reads required auth/analytics/history/strategy tables and returns sanitized dependency states, including maintenance failure. Every response has a server-generated request id. Production request logs are one-line JSON restricted to method, allowlisted route template, status, latency, request id and rate-limit class; queries, unknown path segments, credentials, payloads, player text and IPs never enter logs. Bounded process metrics aggregate status classes and per-method/route counts, errors, latency, slow and limited requests; production access fails closed without an exact 16+ character ops token. Baseline alerts cover dependency failure, 5xx rate, sustained latency and rate-limit pressure with documented actions. P7.5.3 is complete; P7.5.4 is also complete under the local Docker deployment standard described below.

- `docs/英灵城邦战略战役产品审计与阶段规划.md` is the product diagnosis for the strategy pivot. The current implementation is a functional strategy sandbox, not yet a complete campaign. The dependency order is: bounded campaign/end state and monthly clarity (Phase 2), neutral politics/diplomacy/occupation (Phase 3), persistent armies/movement/supply/siege (Phase 4), Snow Ghost world crisis and dynamic story (Phase 5), relic/altar/hero strategy (Phase 6), multiplayer campaign hardening (Phase 7), then replayability/balance/operations (Phase 8).
- Active Phase 2 P2.1 campaign-contract rule: the public HTTP creation path always creates `city_states_twelve_months_v1` with 8 cities, 2 major factions, 6 neutral city-states, a 12-month limit, and a displayed 60–90 minute expectation. `WorldState.campaign_contract` and `campaign_conclusion` persist this without migrating legacy sandbox saves. Unification/elimination concludes early; otherwise month 12 ranks major factions by 100 points per city, average current owner support, 100 active/25 exiled survival points, and 25 per resolved city-battle win capped at 100; unopened mainline contributes 0. Ties compare cities, support, then battle wins and may remain tied. A settled campaign rejects new orders/month advancement until the owner calls `/api/strategy/campaigns/continue-sandbox`; continuation preserves the conclusion, removes the effective month cap, and never repeats the conclusion event. The war room renders contract, remaining months, locked future systems, score breakdown, settlement pause, and continuation control.
- Phase 2 P2.2 monthly information loop is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/monthly_cycle.py`. `WorldState.monthly_reports` keeps the latest 24 structured settlement diffs, while public campaign state removes the raw audit trail and exposes faction-filtered `world.monthly_cycle`. Each faction receives previous-month changes, must-handle food/rebellion/story issues, deterministic per-city income/upkeep/support/rebellion forecasts using the real simulation helpers, and queued-plan timing; war, event-choice, and AI outcomes remain outside the forecast. Queue-action responses include whether the same-key plan was replaced, the previous plan, post-submit command points, faction resource balance, and affected current/next months. The war room renders the three questions “上月发生了什么 / 本月必须处理什么 / 推进后预计发生什么”.
- Phase 2 P2.3 first-campaign guidance is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/campaign_tutorial.py`. Only `city_states_twelve_months_v1` exposes five faction-scoped goals: month 1 border survey, policy plan, and active story choice; month 2 ritual or appointment; month 3 office-appropriate conflict preparation. Current valid queued actions give immediate completion feedback, while successful world events/orders/battles provide durable evidence after settlement. `WorldState.campaign_tutorial` persists only explicit survey acknowledgements and skips; raw progress is removed from public world state. `/api/strategy/campaigns/guide-action` records survey/skip, and skip explicitly grants no resources, consumes no month, changes no rules, and gives no reward. The guide never blocks advancement and legacy sandboxes do not enable it.
- Phase 2 P2.4 office automation is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/office_automation.py` for the bounded first campaign only. Human-controlled factions retain AI-controlled subordinate offices: persistent city policies require no repeat input, while one AI governor may spend 1 command left after player plans to change one unprotected city policy only for food/rebellion emergencies. AI offices never choose high-consequence war, grid battle, or story outcomes without an explicit structured order. Player queue -> human-faction office automation -> other-faction AI -> city settlement is the monthly order. `world.office_coordination` exposes at most three high-consequence decisions, routine city maintenance, and the latest order/request feedback. Queue submission and persisted `OfficeOrder.details` report issuer/executor, 0 request or 1 order command cost, expected month, status, and result. Supported tutorial policy/story/ritual delegations can execute; military and arbitrary text orders are accepted into the desk without automatic world mutation. Legacy sandboxes keep prior behavior.
- Phase 2 P2.5 campaign retrospective is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/campaign_retrospective.py`. A bounded campaign freezes `campaign_conclusion.retrospective` at settlement with major-faction victory/survival/defeat-exile outcomes, up to eight key months, city ownership changes, resolved quick/grid battles, and strategic-hero office/battle experiences. The same snapshot is exposed as `world.campaign_retrospective`. The owner may archive through `/api/strategy/campaigns/archive`, making the campaign permanently read-only, or continue sandbox while preserving the original conclusion and recap. Local allowlisted analytics cover create, lock, enter, months 3/6/9/12, battle trigger, completion, archive, and sandbox continuation without credentials or free text.
- Phase 2 P2.6 visible AI goals are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/ai_goals.py` for the bounded first campaign. Locking initial players immediately assigns every major AI faction one public 2–3 month goal chosen from food/unrest crisis, border defense, ritual reinforcement, army buildup, or a specific viable adjacent capture. `WorldState.ai_strategic_goals` persists current/history; `world.ai_strategic_goals` exposes rationale, deadline, target, progress, last action, and prior-goal change reason. Existing office-authorized AI prioritizes goal-compatible actions, but emergency food/rebellion remains higher priority and all resources, command, adjacency, force, ritual, and office legality still apply. Neutral city-states and legacy sandboxes do not use major-AI goals.
- Phase 2 engineering exit walkthrough is recorded in `docs/Phase2战役走查与阶段复盘.md`. Seed 260 completed the full public HTTP path from month 1 through month 12 assessment and archive without database edits: lord identity, tutorial 5/5, governance, tech, ritual, story handling, one quick city conflict, 11 reports, AI goals, recap, and archive. AI won the assessment 325–300; both major factions survived with one city. The walkthrough fixed office-order queue-key collisions by counting unresolved same-month orders, so distinct commands to the same receiver no longer replace each other. The owner confirmed Phase 2 on 2026-07-18; real-player 60–90 minute usability remains unverified.
- Active Phase 3 P3.1 neutral-politics rule is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/neutral_politics.py`. Each neutral city-state persists `-100..100` relationship scores toward major factions (new campaigns initialize at 0; legacy saves default missing scores to 0). Public `neutral_politics` derives posture, current need, fear source, governor position and relationship bands from live city food forecast, rebellion risk, troops/defense, adjacent major strength, incitement and scores; it never assigns a fixed governor personality. The selected-city war-room command surface exposes the full dossier and the current faction's relationship. Neutral cards remain visible in lord/grand-general workspaces while actions retain office authorization, fixing the prior hidden lord incitement surface. Phase 3 sequencing is recorded in `docs/Phase3实施记录.md`.
- Phase 3 P3.2 neutral diplomacy is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/diplomacy.py`. A major-faction lord may spend 1 command on one replaceable ordinary negotiation per adjacent active neutral per month: aid transfers 80 food/60 money for +18 relation; trade exchanges 40 money for 80 city food and +8; accepted protection transfers 40 money/60 troops for +15 and a structured agreement; accepted non-aggression gives +6 and an agreement; tribute and intimidation resolve from actor border strength or prior intimidation with visible acceptance/refusal effects. Active protection/non-aggression blocks contradictory coercion. Public relationship rows expose all six previews, costs, reasons and effects; the war room renders them while non-lords remain read-only. Incitement remains its target-specific queue and now costs -20 relation toward the instigator.
- Phase 3 P3.3 promise and reputation lifecycle is implemented across `src/wujiang/strategy/diplomacy.py`, `models.py`, `simulation.py`, and `neutral_city_states.py`. Protection/non-aggression lasts 3 months and records fulfilled, treaty-breach, or protection-failure terminal history; friendly action cooldown is 2 months and coercive/incitement cooldown is 3. Major factions persist 0–100 diplomatic reputation starting at 50; aid/fulfillment reward it, coercion/incitement/breach/failure reduce it, and reputation below 30 blocks new protection/non-aggression with a public reason. Structured diplomatic memory, world events, faction memory tags, agreement history/remaining duration, reputation and cooldown locks are exposed in the neutral-city panel. Legacy saves default missing reputation to 50 and infer old agreement expiry from the signing month.
- Phase 3 P3.4 influence and peaceful integration is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/peaceful_integration.py` plus diplomacy, objectives, public-state and war-room surfaces. Each neutral persists independent 0–100 major-faction influence while city `support_by_faction` remains the separate local-population measure; legacy influence defaults to 0. Friendly/coercive diplomacy and fulfillment adjust both values by fixed public amounts. A lord may spend 2 command, 100 money and 80 food to integrate an adjacent neutral without battle only at relation/influence/support 60, reputation 50, one fulfilled agreement and no active incitement; the mortal governor stays, owner support is at least 70, and structured political history persists. Twelve-month assessment adds a capped 100-point influence score from high influence/support in autonomous neutrals plus 25 per peaceful integration.
- Phase 3 P3.5 occupation and rebellion politics is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/occupation.py`, `rebellion.py`, capture/monthly simulation, office authorization, public state and war-room surfaces. A conquest enters a 50%-income, +30-risk pending state until the lord or local governor spends 1 command on autonomy, integration, garrison or plunder; each policy has public immediate and three-settlement consequences before stabilization. Negotiation and local autonomy reduce formal rebels at political/resource costs. Another major lord may fund an occupied/crisis enemy city; sufficiently weak rule can then produce a sourced major-faction defection or restore the original neutral city-state. The lord workspace exposes only the political-crisis card for these decisions and does not inherit governor/general routine controls.
- Phase 3 P3.6 same-rule political AI is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/political_ai.py` and integrated ahead of routine AI spending/expansion. Major AI resolves pending occupation and rebellion crises first, then attempts at most one focused neutral negotiation, peaceful integration, or enemy-resistance funding through the same authorization, command, resource, threshold, cooldown, acceptance and memory functions as players; decisions emit `strategy_ai_political_decision`. Once a live peaceful route has progress, AI keeps a 200-money reserve so routine tech cannot strand the published 100-money integration finish. Fixed first-campaign major factions start with 300 money. Before Phase 4, abstract adjacent attacks consistently commit 75% of the source garrison and evaluate defender troops, defense and local support, exposing the source city after expansion. A lord may issue an exact persistent `set_policy` office order to a directly subordinate AI governor; this preserves the office boundary while making a single-player war economy operable. The 12-month HTTP walkthrough covers player conquest/occupation and rival peaceful integration through final assessment. Phase 3 is complete and owner-confirmed.
- Phase 4 P4.1 persistent armies and form-up are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/armies.py`, `models.py`, office authorization, monthly queue settlement and the General workspace. Each active General can command at most one non-disbanded army with an exact unit inventory, troop-derived manpower, supply/capacity, morale, location, status and order. Formation/reinforcement transfers registered units from the General office and food from an owned garrison city; disbanding at an owned garrison returns both and preserves a `disbanded` historical record. Formation starts at morale 70, requires at least 50 supply and uses capacity `max(200, manpower)`. Host month advancement is a room-level settlement operation and therefore has no Lord-office requirement, while every queued gameplay action is still revalidated against its submitting office.
- Phase 4 P4.2 route movement and ETA are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/armies.py`, `models.py`, `simulation.py`, queued action settlement and the General/map workspace. Every connected node edge costs one strategic month. `set_army_movement` costs 1 command, uses the army id as its replacement key, and supports deterministic shortest-path march/reroute plus halt. Armies persist origin/destination/full route/progress/departure/ETA; month advancement moves each marching army one edge and moves its commander hero with it. Arrival or halt at an owned city yields `garrisoned`, otherwise `deployed`. Supply/morale loss, encounters and siege are deliberately not resolved before P4.3–P4.5. The map shows army badges and highlights remaining route edges; SVG route classes must be applied with `setAttribute`, not `className`.
- Phase 4 P4.3 supply/morale is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/armies.py`, `models.py`, monthly simulation, queued actions and General/map surfaces. After monthly movement, need is `max(10, ceil(manpower / 20)) + 5 per supply-route edge + 5 when marching that month`. A deterministic best owned city supplies through owned intermediate city nodes; distance 0 is local, 1–2 edges open, 3+ strained at half delivery, and a neutral/enemy intermediate node severs delivery. Transport consumes real city food. Full supply restores/holds morale, severed reserves cost 3 morale, shortage costs 12 and increments starvation. Two consecutive shortage months remove the cheapest real registered unit; an empty army becomes `destroyed`. `load_army_supply` lets a General spend 1 command to load food only at the army's current owned garrison city. The map and workspace expose source, route, distance, need, last transfer/consumption and starvation risk.
- Phase 4 P4.4 encounters and pre-battle maneuver are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/armies.py`, `models.py`, `simulation.py`, queued action settlement and General/map surfaces. After retreats and normal movement, hostile armies sharing a node create one persistent `StrategicEncounter`; armies become `engaged` without taking combat losses or changing city ownership. A General spends 1 command to intercept an adjacent enemy after its normal movement, reinforce an adjacent active encounter containing their faction, or retreat an engaged army one safe edge before normal movement at a 10-morale cost. Active non-aggression agreements prevent the applicable major/neutral armies from auto-engaging or intercepting. Encounters end historically when fewer than two hostile sides remain, including retreat or supply destruction. The monthly order is retreat → march → intercept/reinforce → encounter → supply. P4.5 owns siege and P4.6 owns battle resolution/writeback.
- Phase 4 P4.5 persistent siege is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/sieges.py`, `models.py`, `armies.py`, `simulation.py`, queued action settlement and General/Governor/map surfaces. A hostile stationary army at an enemy city starts a persistent `StrategicSiege`; the arrival month never drains or captures. Later months consume real city food and can damage garrison, owner support and fortification. Generals spend 1 command on blockade, starvation, assault or safe withdrawal; Governors spend 1 command on hold, await relief, breakout or surrender. Breakout and zero fortification create P4.6 battle triggers without changing ownership; surrender changes ownership and enters Phase 3 occupation governance. Friendly armies join; defender relief creates a P4.4 encounter, marks the siege contested and pauses erosion. AI surrender requires zero food, severe manpower disadvantage and low support. Monthly order is retreat → march → intercept/reinforce → encounter → army supply → siege. P4.6 owns final battle resolution and army/city writeback.
- Phase 4 P4.6 unified strategic battle resolution is implemented across `src/wujiang/strategy/battles.py`, `battle_adapter.py`, `models.py`, `ai.py`, `web/server.py` and `static/app.js`. A two-sided active encounter, breached assault or breakout can create one source-bound `PendingBattle` with participating army ids, immutable manpower/unit/supply/morale snapshots, node and trigger. Quick, manual, AI-auto and watch-AI all use this record and the same capped 12-soldier roster; persistent armies contribute their real registered units and commander heroes, while siege defenders contribute city garrison/registered units. Resolution writes survivor ratios back to each army, spends battle supply, adjusts winner/loser morale, applies strategic hero sleeping, deterministically retreats defeated armies or destroys trapped remnants, closes encounters, distinguishes assault capture from breakout outcomes, and reuses Phase 3 occupation governance on capture. AI resolves available triggers by the same quick path but yields to already ordered retreats. The old adjacent-city attack remains only as a compatibility path.
- Phase 5 P5.1 crisis state and northern omen are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/world_crisis.py`, `models.py`, `generation.py`, `simulation.py`, `tactics.py` and the strategy war-room UI. Only `city_states_twelve_months_v1` automatically receives `snow_ghost_north_v1`; legacy sandboxes remain unchanged, while old fixed-campaign saves deterministically backfill on public read or month advancement. The minimum `(y, x, node_id)` node is the northern origin and it plus direct neighbors form the stable frontier. Months 1–2 are public dormant preparation; month 3 idempotently activates `omen`, pressure 10, next escalation month 5, a player-visible event and memory tag. Public payloads include localized stage/effect/frontier data; the war room shows the clock and can locate highlighted frontier cities.
- Phase 5 P5.2 border pressure and cold routes are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/world_crisis.py`, `armies.py`, `models.py` and the strategy war-room/map UI. Month 5 idempotently enters `border_pressure`, pressure 30, next escalation month 7; canonical origin-to-direct-frontier edges persist as public cold route keys without deleting graph connections, changing city ownership or spawning Snow Ghost armies. New active movement into a cold edge requires 80 supply: normal marches try a cold-free shortest detour before rejection, while intercept/reinforce reject directly; emergency retreats remain legal. Every real cold-edge traversal by normal/pre-existing march, retreat, intercept or reinforce spends up to 20 supply and then 5 morale when fully paid or 10 on shortage, recording the latest exposure on the army.
- Phase 5 P5.3 spread and persistent Snow Ghost armies are implemented across `src/wujiang/strategy/world_crisis.py`, `models.py`, `armies.py`, `sieges.py`, `battles.py`, `battle_adapter.py`, hero registration and the strategy war-room/map UI. Month 7 idempotently enters `spread`, pressure 60, next escalation month 9; every map edge touching the stable northern frontier becomes a cold route and frontier cities become public threat targets. Exactly one world-crisis faction, synthetic General office and six-unit/600-man Snow Ghost vanguard are created at the origin. A defending field army produces a normal persistent encounter; otherwise the vanguard creates a normal siege, with no scripted capture. `snow_ghost` maps to six real `strategy_snow_ghost` grid units and uses the existing battle writeback. The crisis faction is always hostile but is excluded from major rankings/victory, neutral diplomacy/integration, members, stories, office growth and routine AI; it ignores cold-route exposure, consumes its own internal supply, and does not draw ordinary city food. P5.3 caps at `spread`; month-9 contribution/cooperation/betrayal belongs to P5.4.
- Phase 5 P5.4 mobilization choices are implemented across `src/wujiang/strategy/world_crisis.py`, `models.py`, office/command authorization, queued settlement and the strategy war-room UI. Month 9 idempotently enters `mobilization`, pressure 80, next escalation month 11; during months 9–10 each surviving major faction's Lord may spend one command on one replaceable choice per month. Independent contribution spends 100 food/50 money for 35 contribution. Cooperation spends 80 food/40 money for 25 contribution; reciprocal pledges establish the pair once and grant both factions 15 more contribution, +10 mutual relation and +5 reputation. A later valid betrayal transfers up to 20 contribution, applies -30 mutual relation and -20 betrayer reputation, permanently breaks the pair and records world/faction memory. Crisis state persists contributions, pledge targets, active/broken pairs and decision history; public choices expose costs, targets and legality. P5.4 does not damage Snow Ghosts, alter cities, create the showdown or award mainline victory; P5.5 consumes these inputs.
- Phase 5 P5.5 branching showdown, aftermath and mainline victory are implemented across `src/wujiang/strategy/world_crisis.py`, `models.py`, `battles.py`, `battle_adapter.py`, `objectives.py`, `simulation.py`, the web server and strategy war-room UI. Month 11 idempotently freezes contribution/cooperation inputs into `united_counteroffensive` (active cooperation and total contribution >=80), `rival_vanguards` (no broken cooperation and total >=70), or `shattered_line`; the leader is selected by contribution, reputation, then faction ID among majors with cities. Exactly one `world_crisis` `PendingBattle` is created with branch-specific dedicated registered units, no additional command/resource cost, no city or persistent-army inventory extraction, and the existing quick/manual/AI-auto/watch-AI grid paths. Its dedicated writeback never captures the bound city. Unresolved showdown blocks month 12. Victory clears pressure/routes/threats and destroys crisis armies; united cooperation members with at least 25 contribution share mainline victory, otherwise the leader wins. Defeat enters aftermath, removes 80 food and 10 current-owner support from each threatened city, then permits normal month-12 assessment. Mainline assessment score is faction contribution capped at 100. War-room state persists branch, rosters, room entry, result and shared winners.
- Phase 5 P5.6 same-rule crisis AI and full campaign closure are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/ai.py`, `political_ai.py`, `world_crisis.py`, the web server and war-room UI. From the public month-3 omen onward, optional AI tech/diplomacy/funding spending preserves 50 money and 100 food for mobilization; occupation, rebellion and already-mature peaceful integration may still break that reserve, and no resources are created. During months 9–10 each unclaimed major publicly selects survival, mainline or expansion priority from threatened-city ownership and its existing visible short-term goal. Survival AI proposes/responds to cooperation, mainline AI contributes when legal, and expansion AI may avoid to preserve resources or betray only an already-active cooperation. Every action uses the same Lord, one command, costs and consequences as a player; avoidance grants nothing and persists a public rationale. An AI showdown leader publicly selects the existing quick resolution path immediately after month 11 opens, while a human leader retains control. Domain and HTTP walkthroughs cover participation, avoidance, exploitation, branch persistence, save/resume and month-12 settlement.
- Phase 6 P6.1 relic archives and discovery intel are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/relics.py`, `models.py`, `generation.py`, `tactics.py` and the strategy dossier UI. Only `city_states_twelve_months_v1` enables the system; legacy sandboxes remain empty. Every current strategic hero receives one unique persisted relic with deterministic scattered node, intact/damaged condition, ownership/altar slots, 10-ether maintenance metadata, discovery factions and history. Each major capital registers one separate dormant relic altar with level, capacity, damage, binding and history fields. Each major begins with one deterministic rumor pointing to a neighboring non-owned city; raw relic/altar arrays are removed from public state and the dossier reads faction intel plus unknown counts. The existing ritual site still performs the confirmed 1-command/30-city-ether deterministic random hero summon and requires no relic. P6.1 exposes no search, storage, transfer, repair, binding, maintenance, release, capture or victory action.
- Phase 6 P6.2 relic operations are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/relics.py`, `models.py`, `offices.py`, `command.py`, `web/server.py` and the Lord workspace. A Lord spends 1 command to send one serving, awake hero from its owned city to a known clue on that node or one adjacent node; this consumes 20 city food and the hero's one persisted personal action for the month. Search stores the relic in the origin city and uses a seed/month/hero/relic-stable 25% damage roll. A stored relic transfers only one directly adjacent owned-city edge per month for 10 source food. A damaged stored relic repairs for 40 faction money and 20 storage-city ether. Relic records, city `relics_stored`, history and world events stay synchronized; queue settlement revalidates state before charging. P6.2 still does not expose binding, altar maintenance, release, siege capture, AI relic planning or relic victory.
- Phase 6 P6.3 altar lifecycle is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/relics.py`, `models.py`, `simulation.py`, `offices.py`, `command.py`, `web/server.py` and the Lord/dossier UI. A Lord spends 1 command plus the current level-1 altar's single monthly action to bind one intact, same-city, faction-owned stored relic, or to release one faction-owned bound relic. Entering a new month automatically charges the altar city each bound relic's full maintenance cost (currently 10 ether); insufficient ether causes no partial deduction and puts the altar into visible `damaged`/失养 state while preserving bindings, and a later full payment restores it. Release deterministically relocates and clears ownership/city/altar references while preserving the releasing faction's clue. City storage, relic/altar histories, event logs and queue replacement remain synchronized. P6.3 does not alter ritual-site summons, offices, `ritual_city_id`, siege capture, personal hero consequences, relic AI or relic victory.
- Phase 6 P6.4 hero personal strategy is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/hero_personal.py`, `models.py`, `heroes.py`, `relics.py`, `simulation.py`, `world_crisis.py` and the Lord/dossier UI. Each strategic hero persists bounded loyalty, bounded hero-to-hero relationships, a deterministic specialty, one non-repeatable cross-month personal mission, duty settlement month and recent history. Serving non-Lord heroes must settle two matching duties by start month +3; completion grants +10 loyalty/+8 Lord relation and failure applies -10/-8. Specialty duties yield small real monthly city/army effects. Loyalty deterministically gates non-reserve duties, battle deployment and relic search before submission; resolution never rerolls an already committed battle. Appointment, battle survival/defeat, ritual-city loss and Snow Ghost choices create persisted consequences. P6.4 does not implement relic siege capture, sleeping unbinding, exile linkage, relic victory or relic-planning AI.
- Phase 6 P6.5 city-control relic consequences are implemented across `src/wujiang/strategy/relics.py`, `heroes.py`, `battles.py`, `sieges.py`, `rebellion.py`, `peaceful_integration.py`, `exile.py` and the strategy battle-result UI. Assault, surrender, legacy city attack, rebellion defection/autonomy restoration, peaceful integration, exile rebuilding and hero founding use one control-change writeback. Stored and locally altar-bound relics transfer to the new controller without changing condition or location; the new controller learns them, captured altars spend their current-month action and become damaged when occupied or dormant when empty. A non-Lord hero already sleeping while its relic is lost/released, or defeated after earlier relic loss, keeps the original wake month but loses faction, office and ritual binding; compatibility summon/sleep tags are cleared so save restoration cannot reattach it. Lords retain faction/office identity for exile but lose invalid ritual anchors. Public battle results expose captured relics, disrupted altars and unbound heroes. P6.5 does not implement relic victory or relic-planning AI.
- Phase 6 P6.6 relic victory and same-rule AI are implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/relics.py`, `objectives.py`, `ai.py`, `battles.py`, `models.py` and the strategy map UI. A major faction wins immediately when one intact relic stays bound to its controlled altar and receives three consecutive new-month full maintenance payments; binding month does not count. Progress is keyed by altar/faction/relic and resets on failed maintenance, release, binding mismatch or city control change; it is public with earliest completion month. AI performs at most one relic operation per month through the same Lord permissions and domain functions, responds immediately to owned assets or enemy public progress, begins proactive clue search at month 8, reserves active maintenance ether, and only counters by an otherwise legal advantageous city attack. City capture settles relic/altar ownership before battle-loss hero cloning to avoid a transient invalid state. The seed-268 HTTP walkthrough reaches month 12 with Snow Ghost and AI relic actions together; full regression is 697 passing with 8 environment skips. Phase 6 awaits owner confirmation and Phase 7 remains locked.
- Phase 0 delivery evidence is tracked in `docs/Phase0实施记录.md`. Local privacy-minimized product analytics live in `src/wujiang/web/analytics.py`, write to ignored `var/analytics.sqlite3`, accept only named event/property allowlists, and expose the development funnel at `/api/analytics/funnel`; analytics failures must never block player actions.
- Phase 0 home entry uses three progressively disclosed routes: `快速开始 / 英灵城邦 / 自定义对战`. All playable routes require account login; nickname is display-only and room tokens are recovery credentials. `POST /api/rooms/tutorial-start` creates the fixed `fire_funeral` versus AI `ellie` tutorial room and marks it with `experience_kind=tutorial`.
- Phase 0 onboarding metadata lives in `src/wujiang/web/onboarding.py`: the first selection view shows 10 explained beginner heroes and three server-applied recommended 2-hero rosters, while the complete public roster remains behind an explicit expansion control.
- The fixed Phase 0 tutorial contract is in `docs/新手教学设计.md`: Fire Funeral starts at `(3,4)` against AI Ellie at `(6,4)`, then teaches select, fixed one-cell move, basic attack, Pierce, end turn, one chain response, and free victory. Ordinary mistakes retry in place, refresh restores server-owned progress, and free-fight defeat restores the post-chain checkpoint.
- Tutorial progress is server-owned on `GameRoom.tutorial_state`; `src/wujiang/web/tutorial.py` owns the seven public steps. Tutorial actions are step-gated on the server and filtered in `static/app.js`, mixed-seat AI resolves until human input, and `GameRoom.tutorial_checkpoint` restores the post-chain free-fight state.
- Phase 0 action selection renders a player-facing forecast in `#action-forecast`: resource cost, legal targets, summary-formula basic damage or written skill effect, staged final position, and affected units. Non-highlight board clicks preserve the current action and explain movement, range/line, target, or pattern illegality instead of silently clearing selection.
- Phase 0 real-player validation follows `docs/Phase0内测清单.md`. `/api/analytics/funnel.metrics` exposes first-action median, tutorial/match completion rates, invalid-action rate, median durations, and action attempts. Mobile battle layout must keep the page viewport-width, order tutorial+board before long rails, and confine the 520px board's horizontal scroll to `board-stage`.
- Phase 0 tutorial recovery stores only the most recent tutorial room id under `wujiang-last-tutorial-room` and reuses the existing per-room seat credential. The home quick-start panel verifies server-owned room/tutorial state before offering `继续未完成教学`; completed, invalid, or non-owned rooms fall back to `重新开始教学`, while transient lookup failures keep a retryable explanation instead of inventing local progress.
- The local aggregate review page is `/analytics.html` with data from `/api/analytics/funnel`; it shows empty, loading, refresh, and error states and must never render raw account, session-token, or room-token values.
- Real-player Phase 0 rounds must use `tools/phase0_playtest.py` instead of the shared development database. Each batch isolates analytics and test-account SQLite files under ignored `var/phase0-playtests/<batch>/`, accepts only device-scoped anonymous sample ids plus bounded facilitator observations, refuses accidental overwrite, and reports data readiness separately from the required owner phase-exit confirmation.
- Phase 1 delivery evidence is tracked in `docs/Phase1实施记录.md`. `POST /api/rooms/quick-ai-start` creates an immediate human-vs-easy-AI 2v2: steady-front `masamune + bard` versus ranged-pressure `elite_soldier + excel_r139`, marks the room `experience_kind=quick_ai`, resolves any AI-first actions until human turn/chain input, and returns the selected quick-match metadata. The home quick-start panel keeps tutorial access beside this route, and a finished quick-AI match uses `同阵容再来一局` to create the same configured matchup without returning to draft.
- Phase 1 aggregate analytics add `quick_ai_start` and `rematch_start`; finished quick-AI room ids are recorded once per browser and the local funnel exposes `rematch_within_10m_rate` without storing account or room-token values.
- Phase 1 account history lives in `src/wujiang/web/match_history.py` and ignored `var/match-history.sqlite3`. Each battle start receives a distinct `ROOMID-N` match id, and a finished replay is indexed once per participating account without exposing session or room tokens. `/api/matches/recent` returns the latest 10 account matches; `/api/matches/replay` authorizes the participant and plays the saved JSON even after the live room is gone. Historical replay mode must stop live-room polling and provide an explicit return-to-history path.
- Phase 2 P2.1 account mastery is derived from the same completed match-history rows through private `/api/progression/overview`; do not introduce a second writable counter. Each distinct hero in the account's recorded seat roster earns 1 mastery point per completed match plus 1 on a win, regardless of duplicate unit count. Thresholds are 初识 1 / 熟练 3 / 精通 8 / 专家 15 / 大师 30. Mastery is display-only and never grants gameplay stats. Home and postgame surfaces show the nearest next mastery goal, with explicit empty/loading/error states.
- Phase 1 battle feedback uses `static/battle-feedback.js`: it consumes only viewer-filtered `visual_events` plus public battle-state transitions, keeps the latest three attack/skill, defense, chain, death, and victory messages as icon+text feedback, mirrors them to an assertive live region, and optionally synthesizes short local WebAudio cues after explicit sound opt-in. Sound defaults off. Colorblind high-contrast and motion preferences persist locally; motion defaults to `prefers-reduced-motion`, and all battle controls preserve a 44px minimum target.
- Phase 1 frontend responsibility split is deliberately build-free: `static/home-ui.js` owns recent-match card rendering, `static/replay-ui.js` owns replay-toolbar rendering, `static/battle-feedback.js` owns battle feedback and accessibility preferences, and `static/app.js` retains room/battle orchestration plus board interaction. Keyboard help covers `?`, Escape, Enter, `E`, replay brackets, Space, and arrow-key board-cell focus movement; shortcuts must reuse visible enabled controls and never bypass backend legality.
- Phase 1 hero discovery comes from `/api/heroes.onboarding.hero_discovery`. All 59 public heroes expose their registered role as the filterable position; the 10 beginner heroes keep curated difficulty/position metadata, while the remainder are explicitly marked estimated difficulty from registered mechanism-text length rather than misusing hero level. The lobby's hero picker searches the full public roster by name and sorts by name, level, or any stat; it no longer exposes role/difficulty filters or a beginner-only default view. Room readiness must render the server's localized `can_start` / `start_blocker` result as the hard rule; role-composition advice is non-blocking, and a recommended roster is “applied” only when it exactly matches the currently edited seat.
- The skirmish room lobby shows seats and nothing else. Mode and seat count are read-only facts with a `修改` button that opens `#room-setup-dialog`; the dialog holds a local draft and only issues mode/seat-count/random-size requests on confirm, because a seat-count change really adds or removes seats and a mode change clears every roster. Each seat lists its roster as name-only `hero-tag` chips (delete inline, click to open `#hero-detail`) plus an add button that opens `#hero-picker`. The picker lists heroes as name + level only, searchable by name and sortable by name, level, or any stat, with recommended rosters as chips whose `aria-pressed` state marks an exact match for the edited seat. Server `can_start`/`start_blocker` stays the hard rule and rides on the start button's tooltip rather than a standing paragraph. Room errors go to `state.roomError`, shown in `#lobby-caption`. Focused setup and picker controls count as active room configuration controls so polling does not collapse or replace them mid-input.
- Phase 1 custom rooms require every human seat to confirm readiness after the structural roster rules pass; AI seats are automatically ready, only the host may make the final HTTP start request, and every lobby configuration mutation invalidates all human confirmations. Non-tutorial human prompts use a server-authoritative 90-second timer keyed to the current turn, chain response, or respawn choice; timeout safely ends the turn, skips the chain, or selects the first legal respawn cell. Seat `last_seen_at` drives online/unstable/offline labels without releasing the seat, while the frontend preserves the room token and live state across transient polling failures and reports successful resynchronization.
- Questionnaire generation excludes generic/default/common-rule confirmation questions; durable common rules apply automatically, and the workbook should ask only hero-specific ambiguities not already covered by shared rules. Do not use broad keyword-template rows for resource/duration/summon/frontend categories; questions must be concrete rule alternatives tied to the exact source text, matching the previous single-hero clarification style.
- Future unimplemented Excel heroes use the questionnaire workflow: generate/update `docs/武将实现问题清单.xlsx` with one row per ambiguity question across every unimplemented source-Excel hero, let the user answer that workbook, then implement from the completed answers without further ad hoc per-hero ambiguity passes.
- User clarifications in the current task override older docs and implementation.
- This skill is always-on for this workspace/conversation; use it for every request, even when the request is not obviously gameplay-related.
- Update repo docs before coding when implementing a new hero or durable gameplay rule.
- Use `docs/通用规则.md` for shared battle rules.
- Use `docs/通用技能和特性说明.md` for common skills, traits, and keywords.
- Use `docs/武将说明.md` for per-hero implementation notes.
- Use `docs/武将游戏规则.md` for broad prototype/gameplay rules.
- Use `docs/英灵城邦战略模式规则.md` as the source rule document for the new long-running city-state strategy mode, and `docs/英灵城邦战略模式实现计划.md` for current implementation gaps, staged rollout, and open design questions.
- Strategy office authority is implemented in `src/wujiang/strategy/offices.py`. The fixed v0.1 offices are lord, grand general, general, and governor; players and AI act through offices, never directly through a faction. Every strategic mutation must resolve an authorized office, queued actions persist `issuer_office_id`, and queue settlement revalidates that office.
- Strategy office hierarchy is `lord -> grand_general -> general` and `lord -> governor`. Orders only travel to direct subordinates; requests only travel to direct superiors. Lord/grand-general orders cost 1 command point and subordinate requests cost 0.
- Grand-general capacity starts at 1; general capacity starts at 1 per grand general. Office and staff technologies expand both values. Summoned heroes consume non-lord office capacity even while unappointed, so a full faction cannot perform another ritual until it expands capacity, gains a governor seat, or unbinds a hero.
- Strategy UI has four independent workspaces in `static/app.js`: `LordWorkspace`, `GrandGeneralWorkspace`, `GeneralWorkspace`, and `GovernorWorkspace`. Do not reintroduce a faction-wide command card that mixes governance, summoning, defense, and attacks.
- Role workspaces enforce distinct action surfaces: the lord assigns offices/duties, researches office/unit/building technology, performs rituals, unbinds heroes, and decides faction-level occupation/rebellion crises; the grand general allocates exact registered units to subordinate generals; the general attacks with its unit inventory and must request city units from its parent; the governor manages local policy, troop growth, soldier registration, buildings, local occupation/rebellion decisions, and local rituals.
- Recruitment/求贤 is retired. `src/wujiang/strategy/heroes.py` owns ritual summoning and ritual-site binding. A ritual requires a city ritual site, 30 city ether, one command point, an unbound hero candidate, and free non-lord office capacity. Capturing a city unbinds every hero whose `ritual_city_id` points to that city.
- Cities persist leveled `building_levels` and exact `registered_units`; offices persist exact `unit_inventory`. Infantry costs 100 raw troops per grid unit, archers 140, and cavalry 180. Governor registration uses only locally built and nationally unlocked training types; generals request units and grand generals approve/transfer them.
- Strategy AI in `src/wujiang/strategy/ai.py` must select an AI-controlled authorized office per action. Preserve `strategy_ai_plan` command accounting and write office attribution separately through `strategy_ai_office_trace`.
- New strategic campaigns created through the HTTP player path enable neutral city-states: every major player/AI faction starts with exactly one city, every remaining city is its own `neutral_city_state`, and neutral count must strictly exceed the major starting-city count. Neutral factions use a named mortal governor office, consume no strategic hero, open no faction story event, and run only local policy/defense AI. They never attack unless `incited_against_faction_id` names an adjacent major faction. A major faction lord may queue `incite_neutral_city_state` for 1 command and 60 money; the neutral governor performs one legal quick attack and then clears both incitement fields. Elimination victory compares major factions only, while unification still includes every city.
- Use `references/hero-rule-index.md` for a compact per-hero and per-skill implementation index; keep it synchronized with docs and code.

## Code Map

- `docs/武将补全实施计划.md`: 388-hero completion ledger, H0-H4 execution order, per-hero definition of done, batch gates, current counts, and audit results.
- `src/wujiang/tactical/heroes/excel_roster.py`: the current formal Excel roster gate is `IMPLEMENTED_EXCEL_HERO_CODES`; H1.1 adds `excel_r120`, `excel_r143`, `excel_r172`, `excel_r224`, `excel_r225`, and `excel_r264`. `src/wujiang/tactical/rooms/ai.py` owns their AI scoring/payload behavior, while `src/wujiang/tactical/engine/core.py` carries trusted queued-action metadata for reaction-cost modifiers and exact Evasion outcomes. Aaron reserves a vertical `1*2` entry footprint so his starting Great Unicorn cannot overlap the next compact-lineup unit.
- `src/wujiang/engine/core.py`: generic battle engine, unit state, action queue, chain windows, targeting, shields, damage, healing, field effects, movement, public state.
- `src/wujiang/heroes/common.py`: reusable skills, traits, statuses, selection helpers, line/pattern helpers.
- `src/wujiang/heroes/first_five.py`: first hero batch and their special mechanics.
- `src/wujiang/heroes/next_five.py`: next hero batch. Current implemented heroes include `ElementHunter`, `UndeadKingLina`, `RockGod`, `DoomlightDragon`, `Masamune`, `Jade`, `N`, `BloodEater`, `Li`, `Chanter`, `ErasureApostle`, `DragonRider`, and `SoulWraith`.
- `src/wujiang/heroes/excel_roster.py` plus generated `src/wujiang/heroes/excel_roster_data.py`: source-Excel registration layer for the 370 not-yet-handwritten heroes. It gives stable `excel_r###` hero codes, source stats/raw text, deterministic footprint basics, and only maps pure common skill/trait fragments to real behavior. Special fragments with extra text are implemented through explicit per-hero factories as questionnaire batches are completed; do not silently map them to ordinary common behavior.
- `src/wujiang/heroes/registry.py`: hero registry, battle creation, classic multi-hero spawn/board sizing, random mode, start order. Internal `strategy_*` soldier units are registered for battle creation but filtered from public manual/random hero lists.
- `src/wujiang/heroes/strategy_soldiers.py`: internal v0.1 city-state strategy soldier units used by the strategy battle adapter. These are simple no-skill/no-trait grid combatants for ordinary infantry, garrisons, archers, cavalry, mountain soldiers, ether scouts, and wall engineers.
- `src/wujiang/web/multiplayer.py` and `src/wujiang/web/server.py`: room flow, host mode changes, random roster-size configuration, `2~6` seat ownership/team assignment, seat persistence, room/battle serialization, AI-room simulation control, replay serialization, and replay HTTP endpoints. `multiplayer.py` had legacy overridden `GameRoom` methods removed; the later replay/simulation-aware implementations are the canonical ones.
- `src/wujiang/platform/auth.py`: SQLite-backed registration/login/session module. The default local database is `var/wujiang.sqlite3`, shared with `StrategyStore` unless overridden with `WUJIANG_AUTH_DB`; SQLite files must remain untracked. Login verifies the password before a short `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` session-write transaction and waits up to 30 seconds for competing writers. Persistent SQLite busy/locked errors become a retryable HTTP 503 through `AuthError`, with a player-path regression in `tests/test_behavior.py`.
- `src/wujiang/strategy/quick_campaign.py`: Rescue R1 compact-campaign contract, one-time map/force shaping, public three-choice opening status, authoritative immediate opening effects, and derived at-most-three governance/diplomacy/military recommendations with a readable early expansion window plus the latest faction battle outcome. `static/app.js` owns the quick conclusion surface, continued-sandbox/archive/restart controls and single-player campaign-card wording while the shared strategy backend remains authoritative for settlement and read-only rules.
- `src/wujiang/strategy/models.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/generation.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/store.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/simulation.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/command.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/armies.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/sieges.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/administration.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/story.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/tactics.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/rebellion.py`, `src/wujiang/strategy/battles.py`, and `src/wujiang/strategy/battle_adapter.py`: city-state strategy backend layer. It provides serializable world/city/faction/event/battle/army/encounter/siege models, deterministic connected random city-node maps, SQLite campaign/member/presence persistence, role-gated field levies, local garrison levies and building projects, account-bound multiple campaigns, join codes and resume gating, shared monthly command points, briefings, story events, city settlement, rebellion handling, tactic-tech troop conversion, persistent army movement/supply/siege, source-bound encounter/assault/breakout battle snapshots, compatible quick/real-grid writeback, city attacks, battle-room handoff, and the strategy soldier roster adapter.
