- `src/wujiang/web/ai.py`: room-side AI policy layer. It builds legal move/attack/skill/reaction/instant payloads from battle previews, keeps hidden-info legality by consuming only viewer-legal battle state, and provides per-hero / per-skill scoring hints on top of a generic candidate framework.
- `src/wujiang/tools/match_audit.py`, `src/wujiang/tools/batch_match_audit.py`, `src/wujiang/tools/per_hero_ai_debug.py`, `tools/simulate_match.py`, `tools/audit_implemented_roster.py`, and `tools/per_hero_ai_debug.py`: deterministic local AI-vs-AI audit runners. They reuse the room AI policy, simulate 2-team classic battles, and write traceable reports plus aggregate summaries under `reports/` for Codex-driven hero/AI self-audits.
- `src/wujiang/web/replay.py`: room replay recorder. It stores spectator, per-seat, and omniscient battle snapshots by step, serves viewer-legal replay state, and persists finished battles under `replays/`.
- `static/app.js`: client state, action selection, board rendering, targeting previews, multi-cell click handling, and room/battle orchestration. Recent-match cards, replay-toolbar presentation, and battle feedback/accessibility preferences delegate to `static/home-ui.js`, `static/replay-ui.js`, and `static/battle-feedback.js`; keep those module interfaces narrow and loaded before `app.js`. Dynamic replay-toolbar / zoom-control labels are injected by `ensureDynamicUiScaffolding()` and should stay in Unicode-safe Chinese literals.
- Frontend room-config note: lobby polling must not re-render `renderRoomPanels()` while a seat config control is actively focused (`data-seat-team`, `data-seat-controller`, `data-seat-quota`, seat-count input, random-roster-size input), otherwise native dropdowns collapse before the user can choose an option.
- `static/styles.css` and `static/index.html`: UI layout and presentation.
- `tests/test_battle.py`: battle-engine and hero regression coverage, including turn scheduler and spawn-order assertions.
- `tests/test_multiplayer.py`: multiplayer/session regression coverage.
- `tests/test_behavior.py`: behavior-driven scenarios for public lobby discovery, spectator room visibility, room-to-battle flows, classic and random multi-hero room rules, AI-only room simulation, replay HTTP/control flow, player-facing combat rules, and `quickjs` frontend smoke checks for room-directory rendering/join wiring, random-room roster-size controls, board-overlay reflow, replay toolbar rendering, and battle VFX event consumption in `static/app.js`. Use this layer for future end-to-end-like regressions before adding a full browser automation dependency.
- Current regression coverage explicitly locks two fragile cases: room-AI defensive reactions against stealthed attacks, and fixed-slot turn-order skipping that must still preserve team alternation after a destroyed hero slot is passed.
- `.codex/skills/wujiang-game-rules/references/hero-rule-index.md`: compact index of implemented heroes, skills, traits, summons, and key implementation constraints.

## Current Engine Patterns

- Durable default conventions: do not ask again when these apply. If an active skill has no cost text, it costs 0 mana; if any skill has no use limit/cooldown text, it has no use-count limit; if a damaging skill/area has no fixed `伤 n` and no `没有伤害`, use current attack; if no `破魔` text, it does not pierce shields; if no ally/enemy qualifier, it affects both sides; `单位` includes heroes, summons, and clones; `武将` means non-summon non-clone hero body only; stat reductions to attack/defense/speed/range floor at 1; modifying the `mana` stat means both max mana and current mana are modified then clamped.
- Durable targeting wording: `周围` without extra text means the 8 cells at distance 1 around the unit's occupied cells, excluding its own occupied cells; `攻击` without extra text means basic attack / 普攻, not skills.
- Durable direct-unit targeting rule: skills that directly select one or more units must select targets both within the caster's `范` (or the skill's own written point-unit range, such as `范 5`) and along a horizontal, vertical, or diagonal line from any caster occupied cell to any target occupied cell, unless the skill explicitly overrides this. This includes support walls such as Magic Wall / Light Wall / Stone Wall.
- Durable magic-immunity definition: `魔免` prevents enemy skill damage and enemy skill follow-up effects, but not basic attacks, weather, or field effects.
- Durable common-entry default: if a hero writes a known common skill or trait and adds no extra rule text, implement it exactly as the common version without asking again.
- Excel roster layer status: all 370 source-Excel heroes remain internally registered for development/testing, but public hero lists, manual room selection, and random-room pools include only Excel heroes whose special mechanisms have been individually implemented. Completed special Excel heroes currently include `excel_r020` 妖精王奥尔贝隆, `excel_r021` 过时的完美剑客, `excel_r022` D。黑豹, `excel_r023` 芙蕾, `excel_r024` 不败将军。弗里德, `excel_r025` 代行者。暮别, `excel_r026` 最后的守护者, `excel_r027` 无常, `excel_r028` 飞王, `excel_r029` 红衣, `excel_r030` 聚变人, `excel_r031` 夏目, `excel_r032` 亚伦, `excel_r033` 拉奥魔导士, `excel_r034` 樱火, `excel_r035` 牛鬼, `excel_r036` 制裁者, `excel_r037` 第七神龙。阿尔佐利亚, `excel_r047` 次郎坊, `excel_r056` 蕾米, `excel_r059` 年兽, `excel_r066` 黑猫, `excel_r070` 天蟹, `excel_r071` 雪巨人, `excel_r093` 魔神凯撒, `excel_r094` 杂音, `excel_r113` 诚实天使欧内斯特, `excel_r118` 零崎, `excel_r120` 盲人剑士, `excel_r123` 风魔, `excel_r127` 幻象鸟, `excel_r136` 一刀斩。胧月, `excel_r137` 不死小子, `excel_r138` 无名信使, `excel_r139` 丰收之神。索拉, `excel_r143` 托尔, `excel_r158` 魔战士, `excel_r166` E。电击人, `excel_r172` 甲虫装甲骑士, `excel_r187` 集团恶魔首领, `excel_r188` 光道的守护者。洁拉, `excel_r224` 电子镭射龙, `excel_r225` 电子屏障龙, `excel_r264` 豹头人。飞翼, `excel_r326` 蝴蝶刺客。弗伦萨, `excel_r327` 红莲魔。盖泽尔, `excel_r337` 湿地之主。缇娜, `excel_r352` 水忍, and `excel_r379` 妖仙。菊; 321 Excel heroes remain to complete.
- Durable sheet/default wording: Excel `炎` maps to the existing `火` attribute. Non-square areas such as `3*6` that do not say `身前` are remote range-based rectangles and can be placed in either orientation. Text like `攻击带有以下破魔效果` or `带有破魔的 X 效果` makes only the listed / named follow-up effect piercing, not the whole attack or skill.
- Durable passive-text default: only skills whose text starts with / explicitly says `被动技能` are passive chain skills. Other skills are not passive unless they say `随时使用` or another timing rule.
- Durable ultimate-prefix default: skills prefixed with `￥` or `¥` are big moves / ultimates. They default to once per battle unless the skill text writes a different use count, cooldown, or reset.
- Durable passive-trigger default: passive skills without extra support/protection/other trigger wording can only chain when their owner is about to be affected by an enemy action.
- Durable magic-point default for new heroes: if a future hero has a magic-point mechanic and no explicit initial magic-point value, its initial magic points equal its base mana.
- Durable n-v-n timing rule: one turn belongs to one hero, not one player. One player may control multiple heroes, but only one hero acts in a turn. A hero's `己方回合` means that hero's own turn, and one `轮` for that hero runs from its own turn start to its next own turn start.
- Durable start-phase / toggle rule: each hero turn has a start phase. Resolve start-of-turn effects first, then in that same start phase ask whether to use start-phase toggle skills. Toggle skills alternate open/close on each use and become unavailable after normal movement, attacks, or other active actions.
- Durable cannot-act rule: `不能行动` means the unit cannot normal move, cannot basic attack, cannot use active skills, and cannot use passive chain skills.
- Durable split-movement rule: `没有移动次数限制` means a unit may split normal movement into any number of normal moves, but total normal movement distance in that turn still cannot exceed current speed.
- Durable ordered-path rule: normal movement submits a full ordered adjacent-cell path. Within the current speed budget it may turn, backtrack, return to its origin, and repeat cells. A pass-through trigger counts entry into a unit footprint; continuous overlap across several cells of a multi-cell unit is one crossing, and a new crossing requires fully leaving that footprint before re-entry.
- Durable passage-damage rule: `穿人有伤害` is generic. Finish movement first, then queue one fixed-cell damage and independent chain window per recorded footprint entry, in path order. Repeated entries into one cell remain separate windows while a hostile occupies it; after Evasion leaves that cell, later instances no longer ask that unit to react and hit only current occupants. Zerozaki's `穿人魔+0.5` is a separate trait, granted per completed crossing even if a later hit misses, is shielded, or destroys the target.
- Durable extra-move-count rule: `每回合移动次数 +1` grants one extra normal-move action per turn; each normal move is still separately capped by current speed.
- Durable round-duration rule: if docs/rules say an integer `持续 N 轮` for a unit status or integer-round local effect, count it on the affected unit's or owner's own round boundary, not on every global hero turn. Half-round effects such as `1.5轮` / `2.5轮` still need explicit per-skill handling.
- Durable passive-count rule for n-v-n: passive/reaction skills written as `每回合最多 X 次` reset separately in each opposing hero turn, not once per whole round. Mana-costed passives still pay cost on each use. Free passives such as Ion Shield follow the same per-opponent-turn reset; Quantum Shield additionally keeps its own round-based lockout rule.
- Durable classic-room rule: `classic` is the fixed-spawn two-player multi-hero mode. Each side may pick duplicate heroes and an uncapped roster size; summons, clones, and mounts never enter the global turn ring.
- Durable random-room rule: `random` is also a two-seat multi-hero mode. The host sets `n`; when the battle starts, each side receives `n` random heroes sampled without repetition across the whole battle, so the same hero cannot appear twice on the field in one random match.
- Durable multi-seat room rule: online rooms use `2~6` seats but still only two teams. The host manually assigns each seat to red or blue; seat states are `open / human / AI`; a room cannot start while any seat remains open or if there is no human seat.
- Durable account rule: all current playable room modes require login before creating or joining a room. Room tokens remain seat-recovery credentials only; they do not replace account login.
- City-state strategy mode status: rule docs and an implementation plan are in `docs/`, auth endpoints/UI exist, and the bounded twelve-month strategy campaign is implemented through the first complete Snow Ghost world mainline. Confirmed strategy direction is multiplayer room-based campaigns, resumable only by prior participants, and resume may continue only when all human initial players are online; when the host locks initial players, unclaimed starting factions become AI-controlled campaign members and do not count against the human resume gate. One account may own multiple campaign saves. Current backend includes core world/city/faction/event/battle models, deterministic connected random maps, account-bound campaign persistence, join codes, pre-lock member joining, host-only join-code rotation, host-only initial-player locking with AI filler for empty factions, member permission checks, presence, `/api/strategy/campaigns*` endpoints, monthly settlement, city policy changes, tactic-tech unlocks, city-flavored troop conversion, persistent armies/movement/supply/encounters/sieges, compatible grid-battle writeback, neutral politics, occupation/rebellion, and the complete Snow Ghost crisis through branching showdown and aftermath. Current frontend homepage strategy panel includes the compact map, office workspaces, campaign state, persistent military intel and public crisis/AI intent display. Relics, altars, siege-specific grid terrain, finer multiplayer role handoff, full invitation/revocation workflows beyond join-code reset, complete postbattle relic/officer writeback, and a full interactive strategy map page remain later work.
- City-state strategy queued-action rule: `strategy_actions` persists current-month player plans. Players may queue city policy changes, tactic-tech unlocks, rebellion handling actions (`appease`, `relief_grain`, `suppress`), sandbox rebel-clearing battles (`rebellion_battle`), city-bound strategic hero recruitment, and city attacks using `manual`, `watch_ai`, `ai_auto`, or `quick` resolution; actions are keyed per campaign/user/month/type/action key so repeat plans replace earlier ones. City-bound actions are limited to 2 orders per city per month for the faction, while replacing an existing same type/key action does not consume another slot. Host month advancement applies queued actions in submission order before monthly settlement, creates queued real-grid rooms when needed, runs queued `ai_auto` rooms to completion server-side, logs invalidated actions instead of aborting the month, marks the old month's queue resolved, and returns an empty current-month queue plus any battle rooms created during advancement.
- City-state strategy command rule: each faction, including AI, shares 4 command points per month; player factions also retain the 2-order city cap. Policy, tech, summon, ordinary rebellion handling, and exile actions cost 1; attacks, rebel-clearing battles, and suppression cost 2. Replacing the same action type/key recalculates that slot instead of charging twice. AI reserves points for an available attack instead of spending past the cap. Public campaign state exposes used/remaining points, and public world state exposes deterministic per-faction threat/opportunity/rival-intent briefings.
- City-state story-event rule: each non-exiled faction has at most one pending event, selected deterministically from the campaign seed/month/faction while avoiding a template used in the previous two months. Player and AI choices cost 1 command point and use the same resource validation. Unanswered events take a hidden default outcome on the next month, while selected/default choices may schedule persistent delayed consequences. Choices and consequence resolution write world-memory tags and event-log entries. Initial templates are border deserters, grain petition, ether flare, and guild dispute.
- City-state strategy rebellion rule: monthly settlement stores the current risk as `rebellion_risk:<risk>:<stage>`. Risk 45+ records a `rebellion_crisis` marker and event. Risk 75+ triggers formal rebellion: `rebellion_force` is created or grows, city troops/food/money/support are damaged, local autonomy support rises, and `rebellion_uprising` is logged. Suppression and sandbox rebel-clearing battles remain available; negotiation spends 60 money and cuts rebels 40%, while granting autonomy cuts rebels 70% in exchange for local-autonomy growth. External major-faction funding spends 60 money, adds 120 rebels and leaves a source. When owner support is at most 15 and rebels match the garrison, sufficient sponsored/unsponsored major support can cause defection; otherwise autonomy support 80 can restore the original neutral city-state. Political resolution clears the rebellion once and records the outcome. Rebel real-grid battles remain future work.
- City-state strategy monthly AI rule: after queued player actions and before monthly settlement, host month advancement runs deterministic AI for factions not controlled by human campaign members. AI campaign members created during strategy lock are display/seat records only and must not be counted as human-controlled factions. Each non-exiled unclaimed major faction handles the P3.6 political chain plus public short-term goals, city policy, affordable tech, hero ritual/defense, persistent-army/siege triggers and legal attacks. During Snow Ghost mobilization it first chooses a public survival/mainline/expansion priority and uses the same Lord, command, resource, cooperation and betrayal rules as players; avoidance has no hidden reward. An AI showdown leader immediately selects the normal quick-resolution entry after month 11 opens. AI logs `strategy_ai_plan`, `strategy_ai_political_decision`, crisis intent and applicable office/military traces; it skips player-controlled and exiled factions. Relic release and deeper relic-aware AI remain later-phase work.
- City-state strategy objective/exile rule: public `strategic_status` evaluates city-count, elimination, exile, Snow Ghost world-mainline victory and relic-altar victory on every payload. Implemented victory conditions are `unify_cities`, `eliminate_enemy_factions`, `world_mainline`, and fixed-campaign `relic_altar`; world-mainline and relic victory do not require city unification. Month-12 assessment reads faction contribution capped at 100. Month advancement records idempotent exile/victory events with memory tags. Exiled factions retain the existing aid, followers, network and rebuild-base actions. The frontend renders objective/exile status, public altar progress and current legal exile actions; richer exile diplomacy/vassalage remains future work.
- City-state strategy hero rule: `strategic_hero_pool` is generated dynamically from `list_heroes()`, so current playable handwritten heroes and implemented Excel heroes automatically enter the strategy layer, while internal `strategy_*` soldiers and unimplemented Excel rows stay excluded. Each campaign deterministically assigns every strategic hero to a home faction from `world.seed` plus hero code. Factions expose their `strategic_heroes` slice, `strategic_hero_deployment_limit`, and players may queue `summon_strategic_hero`; month advancement pays `20 + level * 5` ether, records `strategic_hero_summoned:{code}` in faction memory, and logs `strategic_hero_summoned`. V0.1 city battles let the attacker choose summoned non-sleeping strategic heroes for immediate or queued attacks up to the dynamic faction deployment limit; the selection is stored on `PendingBattle.attacker_hero_codes`. Default limit is 1; `hero_command` / 英灵军议 adds +1 and makes the frontend render multi-select checkboxes. Players may set one default defender with `strategic_hero_defender:{code}` through `/api/strategy/campaigns/set-defense-hero`; `PendingBattle.defender_hero_codes = None` means use that default if still valid, otherwise fall back to the automatic defender default. The defending faction may set or clear a specific pending battle's defender heroes through `/api/strategy/campaigns/set-battle-defense-hero` until a real grid `battle_room_id` is attached; this writes `PendingBattle.defender_hero_codes` and makes the roster explicit. Committed heroes enter manual/watch-AI/AI-auto rosters as extra real hero codes; defeated committed heroes record `strategic_hero_sleeping:{code}:until:{month}` and show `sleeping` until the recovery month, then return to `summoned`. Hero missions, relic binding, and officer roles are future work.
- City-state strategy design decisions: v0.1 defaults are implementation-chosen reasonable sandbox values, currently 8 cities and 2 factions; all victory categories remain target scope; no-city failure continues as exile; multiplayer roles must later split beyond host/member; tactic tech should branch like 4X/grand-strategy infantry, archery, cavalry, engineering/siege, ether/scouting, logistics, and city-defense trees; strategic hero pools include all current hero pools and must automatically include future added heroes.
- Durable seat-ownership rule: a seat actively controls only its own heroes, but same-team seats may still legally use support reactions, walls, protection, knockback, and instant skills to help allied heroes owned by other seats.
- Durable multi-seat team-order rule: in multi-seat team rooms, ignore seat order when building the battle turn ring. Merge all red-team heroes into one sorted list and all blue-team heroes into one sorted list, then alternate `red1 -> blue1 -> red2 -> blue2 ...`; if one side runs out, the other side finishes its remaining fixed slots.
- Durable multi-seat random rule: `random` keeps `n vs n` at the team level. If a team has multiple seats, the host must configure a per-seat random-hero quota whose sum equals that team's `n`.
- Durable AI rule: AI must use only legal viewer information and must not peek hidden information such as stealth, clone truth, or unrevealed statuses. Difficulty has three levels: `easy / standard / aggressive`, with a room default plus optional per-seat overrides. When choosing an attack or hostile skill, AI assumes the opponent does not chain and filters out candidates that cannot damage, consume shield/resources, or apply an effective effect to at least one enemy; consuming a normal shield or leftover temporary shield from Protection-like effects counts as effective impact; multi-target candidates remain valid if at least one enemy would be affected. AI must not generate movement-skill candidates while the acting unit cannot move / cannot use movement skills, should avoid evasion destinations that remain in the queued action's declared damage area, and should not spend an action on a hostile effect whose follow-up materially benefits the enemy unless the immediate damage/control value justifies it. For voluntary active/instant skills that have no per-turn, per-battle, or cooldown limit and do not require enemy impact, AI may use the skill but throttles repeat use after the first use by that same actor in the same turn so utility skills such as stealth/clone/summon/buff tools do not drain all mana.
- Durable AI turn-bundle rule: AI turn decisions must evaluate every unit that can act in the current hero's turn bundle, including eligible summons, clones/copies, and knight mounts, instead of only the hero body. If the hero body has no useful action, AI should still use useful actions from those controlled units before ending the turn.
- Durable collision-movement AI rule: AI must score movement-caused effects from the submitted path, not only the destination. Zerozaki should deliberately leave and re-enter enemy footprints for repeated pass-through damage when worthwhile; Judgment Stone should value an enemy-occupied movement endpoint by its resulting `5*5` explosion and friendly-fire cost.
- Durable Kiku AI/death rule: `sun_slash` directly targets an enemy within Kiku's current range and the standard straight-line targeting rule; it is both damaging and controlling for AI scoring, with once-per-battle conservation. Kiku's death grants the independent fixed-4 legacy attack to every other allied unit currently on the field, including summons and clones, but not units summoned later.
- Durable AI start-toggle rule: a beneficial zero-cost start-phase toggle must outrank ordinary attacks and skills when its prerequisites are met, because taking an ordinary action closes the toggle window. Sakura specifically opens `floating_cannon_berserk` before evaluating cannon or body attacks whenever she has a live cannon and an enemy.
- Durable conditional-reset AI rule: prerequisite-gated reset skills need an explicit usable-state score instead of the generic self-buff fallback. 牛鬼 prioritizes `mountain_awakening` once 8 `山神计数点` are present.
- Durable batch-013 AI rule: movement follow-ups such as `jirobo_follow_step`, destination-centered damage such as `remi_chaos`, turn-ready summons such as `remi_bat`, and direct unit control skills such as `nian_roar` must expose payloads that survive legality and effective-impact filtering. Resource restoration skills with meaningful cooldowns need waste guards; 年兽 does not use `nian_dragon_dance` at full hp merely to restore 1 missing mana.
- Durable batch-014 AI rule: nested choice skills must submit every player choice explicitly. `heaven_punishment` payloads contain area cells, target unit, and selected public active skill; already sealed choices may exist as raw diagnostic payloads but are removed by effective-impact filtering. Its audit diagnostic treats zero preview targets as an expected no-payload state even when remote area patterns exist. Start-phase toggles and once-per-battle weather setup also need state-aware scores: 黑猫 evaluates `black_cat_form` from current skill threats and attack access, while 雪巨人 values `big_avalanche` only while its weather is absent.
- Durable 牛鬼 AI/trigger rule: `山神计数点` from a failed basic attack requires `enemy_reacted` and no enemy damage from that attack; innate immunity or another non-chain cancellation does not grant a point. At 8 points, AI prioritizes `mountain_awakening` so the reset is not stranded behind generic buff scoring.
- Durable mounted movement rule: if a skill explicitly moves a mount and carries the rider, that movement belongs to the mount. Masamune's mounted free `飞跃` is not mount movement; it is Masamune's own free Leap while mounted, so Motor Horse stays in place and the mounted state clears when Masamune lands outside the horse footprint.
- Durable AI payload rule: direction-selection skills and basic attacks expose `selection.mode="direction"` plus a direction list and AI must submit a direction payload, not an arbitrary cell. Reaction AI must not create an empty base payload for target/cell-requiring reactions when preview exposes no legal cells or targets, and evasion AI must avoid cells already reserved by prior evasion reactions in the same chain window. Active-turn AI attack payloads and target-sensitive skill payloads such as `heaven_punishment` must still pass `Battle.build_queued_action(...)` legality before scoring, even when they were generated from backend preview cells/patterns. Audit tooling treats an available attack preview with no target ids as an expected no-payload state, not a high-signal generation gap.
- Durable AI copied-skill rule: wrapper skills such as D。黑豹's `mimic_skill` must generate nested payloads for the copied skill's own targeting requirements, then score and enemy-impact-filter through the copied skill instead of treating the wrapper's selected unit as the final effect target.
- Durable AI reaction-target rule: reaction payload builders must ignore stale preview unit ids for units that have already died, disappeared, or left the board during the current chain, and should still build legal shield payloads for remaining live preview targets.
- Durable AI-audit workflow: use `python tools/simulate_match.py --team1 a,b --team2 c,d --seed N --max-steps M` to generate traceable reports under `reports/`. Findings are audit leads, not automatic rule verdicts; compare each finding against the hero source text/questionnaire before changing rules or code.
- Durable per-hero AI-debug workflow: for every implemented hero being audited, and after every new hero implementation, read that hero's implemented source/docs plus questionnaire answers if available. If older questionnaire answers were not preserved, record that they are unavailable. Run `python tools/per_hero_ai_debug.py --targets hero_code --matches-per-hero 10 --seed N --max-steps M` so the target hero is paired with one random implemented teammate against two random implemented opponents, varying seeds and teams. Compare battle reports, structured findings, hero text, and questionnaire/source clarifications; record suspected hero-rule and AI defects in the generated traceable `reports/per-hero-ai-debug/` artifact with hero, seed, teams, report path, observed behavior, expected behavior, source evidence, severity, and proposed fix. Fix recorded defects and rerun targeted tests plus enough simulations to verify.
- Durable AI-audit batch workflow: use `python tools/audit_implemented_roster.py --seed N --rounds R --max-steps M` to cover the current public/selectable implemented roster and aggregate high-signal findings.
- Durable hero-implementation validation workflow: review three heroes per batch by default, while preserving the complete design-first sequence independently for every hero. Lock every batch member's source-only design baseline before inspecting any member's battle code, AI, or tests. Source ambiguities are resolved by a recorded design inference (decision, rejected alternatives, evidence, confidence, consequences) rather than a user pause. After implementation or modification, set the one-to-three explicit `current_hero_codes` in `docs/武将符合性审查.json`; the user runs `python detail_test_all_wujiangs.py --current`, which keeps the complete ordinary regression but limits each 10-match deep audit to that batch. The flagless command remains the phase-level full-roster audit. Fix both hero mechanics and AI behavior surfaced by the focused report, including general AI logic and hero-specific payload/scoring logic, then report remaining high-signal findings and next targets.
- Durable queued-target rule: direct unit skills resolve against the declared target cell after reactions. If the originally selected unit leaves that cell and no valid target remains there, the skill misses and logs a miss instead of retargeting by unit id or raising a range error.
- Durable queued-area rule: declared area/basic attacks resolve against the originally declared area cells after reactions. If every target leaves those cells, the attack misses and logs a miss instead of recomputing the area from the post-reaction board or raising a no-target error.
- Durable shield-reaction rule: shield reactions should not be offered for a target that already has a shield that would auto-block the queued action, but multiple shield reactions that were legally declared in the same chain may still resolve/stack on that target.
- Durable chain-reaction invalidation rule: if a passive/reaction skill user dies, is removed, or leaves the board during reaction declaration side effects, the declared reaction is skipped as invalidated and the chain continues/finalizes instead of raising an action error or stalling the pending chain.
- Durable forced-pull rule: chain-pull style forced displacement, including Dragon Slash's chain effect, is limited by the skill line/landing legality, not by the target unit's own speed stat.
- Durable replay rule: every completed battle should auto-save a replay under a repo-local `replays/` directory until manually deleted. Replay defaults to legal viewer perspective and may switch to omniscient only after the battle ends. Required controls are pause/resume, speed change, step forward/backward, and timeline scrubbing.
- Durable AI-spectator rule: if no currently present hero belongs to a human seat, the room auto-enters AI simulation / spectator mode. This supports rooms where a human host stays as a pure spectator seat with zero heroes while AI seats fight for both teams.
- Durable timeout-win rule: in every mode, if the battle still has no winner after `20 * initial hero count` completed hero turns, immediately choose a random winning side. `Initial hero count` means the opening non-summon heroes only; summons, clones, and mounts do not increase the cap.
- Implementation compatibility rule: room / roster classic mode uses the multi-hero alternating turn ring, but direct `create_battle("hero1", "hero2")` calls still keep the legacy fixed `8x8` duel path and player-turn bundle semantics used by older engine tests and local helpers.
- Durable classic turn-order rule: at battle start, sort each side's heroes once by `speed desc -> level desc -> attack desc -> defense desc -> range desc -> mana asc -> random`. Compare each side's first hero to decide which side starts, then interleave the two side lists in alternating order for the rest of the battle. Destroyed heroes keep their slot and are skipped; temporary disappear/banish does not remove the slot and must attempt return first.
- Durable classic spawn rule: classic board size and fixed spawn slots depend on both roster size and opening footprint sizes, including mounted-entry or future oversized heroes. Do not hard-code classic mode to 8x8 or assume only 1x1 / 1x2 / 2x2 entries. Spawn slots are assigned by the precomputed classic turn order, not by lobby pick order.
- Durable random spawn rule: `random` reuses the same board-size calculation and interleaved turn-order rules as `classic`, including opening footprint sizing for mounted-entry or future oversized heroes, but replaces fixed symmetric spawns with random per-side spawns inside fair left/right bands.
- Summon default stats: omitted summon stats default to attack 1, defense 1, speed 1, range 1, mana 0.
- Knight durable rule: the first summon in a Knight hero's skill text is the mount unless explicitly overridden. Default mount footprint is vertical `1*2`, and mounts default to the `可乘骑` trait.
- Knight durable rule: Knight heroes enter battle already having summoned their mount and already mounted unless the hero text overrides that. A rider can have only one own mount on the field at once; when that mount is destroyed, the rider must wait through one own turn before summoning it again.
- `可乘骑` durable rule: if mounted unit `a` is ridden by unit `b`, only `a` receives damage and skill effects; when `a` is threatened, `b` can still chain for `a`, and support reactions such as Protection or Knockback resolve on `a`. Mounted state ends when `a` uses a normal move or movement skill to leave `b`.
- `可乘骑` also redirects point targeting: clicking rider `b` still causes the damage or skill effect to resolve on mounted unit `a`.
- Mounted movement durable rule: when mounted unit `a` moves, rider `b` is carried along. Rider `b` may still move independently; if `b` leaves `a`'s occupied cells, mounted state ends immediately.
- Mounted AI durable rule: AI should not voluntarily choose rider-body normal movement or generic rider movement skills while mounted, because that dismounts and splits the knight from the mount. It should keep the rider fighting from the mount and use mount movement for repositioning. Masamune's mounted-only free `飞跃` is an exception granted to the rider himself: it moves Masamune, not Motor Horse, and normally dismounts if he leaves the mount footprint.
- Current implementation files for Knight / mount behavior:
- `src/wujiang/engine/core.py`: mounted overlap rules, effect-recipient redirection, rider proxy reactions, payload-aware basic attacks.
  - `src/wujiang/heroes/next_five.py`: `Masamune`, `MotorHorseSummon`, `DragonRider`, `DragonMountSummon`, mount cooldowns, direction-declared / area-declared basic attacks, free mount `Shensu`, mounted free `Leap`, and `Jade` local-field / use-window mechanics.
  - `src/wujiang/heroes/registry.py`: random-mode spawn uses `entry_footprint_*` so mounted-entry heroes reserve mount space.
  - `static/app.js`: board targeting for attack variants, attack direction choice UI, mounted overlap-aware movement highlighting, mounted rendering order with rider above mount, and sidebar panels that must remain manually expandable even while waiting on the opponent or a chain.
- Mana stat is both spawn mana and mana cap. Current mana clamps to `Unit.max_mana()`.
- `Skill.timing == "instant"` is live. Instant skills can be used during the owner's own turn unless their own text limits them to the opposing turn, and can be used directly during an opposing turn or in an existing chain window. No default self-impact trigger applies; only explicit skill text such as `在……时` or a named situation adds a trigger condition. They have chain speed `3`, which the opponent cannot counter-chain, and do not count as `performed_active_skill`.
- `StatusEffect.tick_scope` supports `owner_turn_start`, `owner_turn_end`, and `any_turn_end`. Use `owner_turn_start` for integer-round statuses that should expire at the owner's next-round boundary instead of after every global turn.
- Viewer battle state may expose `active_units` to a waiting player with a legal instant skill. `GameRoom.perform_action(...)` allows that skill to bypass the normal `input_player` gate after seat and skill legality checks; an existing chain window instead uses `chain_react`.
- `Battle.peek_next_turn_unit()` computes the next alive non-summon hero in the fixed turn ring, and `Battle.to_public_dict()` exposes `next_turn_unit_id`, `next_turn_unit_name`, and `next_turn_player_id` for frontend turn-preview UI.
- `BattleFieldEffect.on_turn_start(...)` is available for start-of-owner-round cleanup or triggers on local fields that should not use global `duration` countdowns.
- Multi-seat implementation note: keep battle `player_id` team-based when possible, but model room seats and seat-owned hero control separately in the room layer. Active-turn permissions should be seat-owned, while same-team support reactions remain allowed across seats.
- Current room implementation note: human seats now own only their own heroes even inside the same battle-side team. Multi-seat room start builds battle heroes with `owner_seat_id`, filters `active_units` by seat, and keeps hidden-info visibility team-based.
- Current room implementation note: AI seats are live in battle now. `GameRoom._resolve_ai_until_human_input()` uses `src/wujiang/web/ai.py` to play active turns, reactions, respawn choices, and instant-skill interrupts for seat-owned units. When no human-owned hero remains present, `GameRoom` auto-advances the room as a paced AI simulation, records replay steps through `src/wujiang/web/replay.py`, exposes `/api/rooms/replay` and `/api/rooms/simulation-control`, and lets the host pause / step / scrub live AI-only battles from the frontend replay toolbar. AI candidate scoring for area payloads should reuse payload cells instead of rebuilding expensive pattern lists for every candidate.
- AI simulation recovery note: if a staged AI action reaches confirmation but its queued actor has left the battlefield or the staged payload has become illegal, room simulation must clear the staged action, record a failed AI replay step, and fall back to chain skip / respawn fallback / end-turn fallback instead of leaving AI stalled or throwing out of `/api/rooms/state`. Detect stale queued actions from `Battle.stale_queued_action_count`, not from localized log text, so hidden logs and any skill name such as `吸魔` recover the same way. If stale queued-action resolution leaves the current turn parked on a unit that can no longer act, the room may force an end-turn fallback even when that stale unit belongs to a human seat; do not auto-act for a human unit that can still act.
- Replay implementation note: prefer event log plus periodic snapshots so rewind/scrub stay responsive without storing a full state for every frame.
- `DamageContext.preserve_followup_effects` is the engine flag for “this hit's damage was prevented, but the rest of the same effect should still apply”.
- `Unit.allow_unbounded_mana` lets a hero's current mana and displayed mana cap grow beyond base mana.
- `Unit.footprint_offsets` supports rectangular and irregular multi-cell units. Use `battle.unit_cells(unit)`, `battle.can_place_unit(...)`, `battle.distance_between_units(...)`, and `battle.unit_distance_to_cell(...)` instead of direct `unit.position` comparisons when a rule may interact with multi-cell units.
- `Unit.set_footprint_cells(...)` and `Unit.set_footprint_offsets(...)` keep dynamic body shapes on the same anchor; a unit's `position` is an anchor, not necessarily an occupied cell after body-shape skills.
- `Unit.allow_overheal` allows specific heroes to heal above `max_health`; default healing still caps at `max_health` for everyone else.
- Direct attacks and point-target skills against multi-cell units must declare an actual occupied target cell, not blindly use the unit anchor.
- Multi-cell movement must validate the destination footprint, not only the anchor/top-left cell. Non-stealthed units cannot overlap other non-stealthed units after movement.
- Occupancy is stricter than before: no unit may end movement or be placed onto another unit's occupied cells unless a rule explicitly grants overlap. Stealth alone never grants overlap. Current explicit exceptions are mount/rider overlap and Ellie's Medusa summon onto a cell occupied only by a stealthed unit.
- Ellie Medusa special case: summoning Medusa onto a cell with only stealthed occupants must not open a chain window for that hidden unit, but it does create a temporary overlap. The stealthed unit is then blocked by the shared-cell stealth rule until it moves away.
- Range damage against multi-cell units should set `DamageContext.area_cell_hits=battle.unit_hit_count_for_cells(unit, cells)` so each extra occupied cell hit adds +1 attack power.
- Full pierce uses `ignore_shield`; half pierce uses `half_ignore_shield`.
- Shields block chain options unless the queued action has `ignore_shield` or `half_ignore_shield`.
- Reaction windows order all units that can currently chain by current speed descending, then level descending, then random order among exact ties.
- Each skill effect is judged independently for reactions: if that effect would affect an opposing unit, that unit can potentially chain. Multi-effect damage/effect skills must open a separate reaction window for each hostile effect segment. A single effect may contain multiple resolution results, and those results share one reaction check. Effect boundaries are semantic: Paralyzing Glove, Complete Burn, Blizzard, Judgment Fire, and Mana Pull are currently single-effect composites, not split effects. Wind Sand, Backstep Shot, and Fate Kick are multi-effect skills, but their weather/backstep/dash first stages usually do not affect opposing units for now; future speed-3 reactions can chain to a first effect if it does affect an opposing unit.
- Code structure: `QueuedAction` supports `skill_effect` actions with optional `effect_resolver`; use `build_skill_effect_action(...)`, `queue_skill_effect_action(...)`, or `queue_area_damage_effect(...)` for explicit effect stages. Wind Sand weather, Backstep Shot retreat/counter, Fate Kick dash/banish, and Rock Cannon impact damage are represented as explicit effect stages; the first three currently preserve the old UX by resolving their non-hostile or direct-reaction stages without opening extra windows.
- `Battle.current_action_resolution_token` identifies the one queued action currently being resolved. Every effect, damage hook, and follow-up executed inside that declaration sees the same token; a nested queued declaration receives its own token and the outer token is restored afterward. Traits whose contract says "once per declared action" must deduplicate by this token rather than by log text, effect name, target count, or action-history length. `Battle.record_rule_trigger_summary(...)` is the reusable audit hook for recording such triggers with the current token. Cat Uncle uses it for structured `rule_trigger` evidence; the match audit treats more than one Cat retaliation event sharing one token as high-signal `duplicate_once_per_action_trigger`, while distinct nested-action tokens remain legal.
- Weather is represented as a `BattleFieldEffect` with `weather_name`; use `battle.unit_in_weather(name, unit)` for unit-specific effects because weather may be local, and `battle.has_weather(name)` only means some weather effect of that name exists. Full-board weather effects set `global_weather = True`; local weather effects must return concrete `affected_cells`.
- Same-name weather does not stack. If a unit/cell is covered by multiple weather effects with the same `weather_name`, including local plus global weather, damage and restrictions are applied once.
- Weather description source: `data/武将yoo.xlsx` latest-hero column M now supplies an explicit effect for every weather-related source hero. `tools/sync_weather_descriptions.py` regenerates `src/wujiang/tactical/heroes/weather_effects_data.py` and `docs/武将天气效果清单.md`; roster, lobby, hover, and battle details expose `weather_effect_text`. Audit as of 2026-10-09: five source-table weather types are implemented (沙尘, 大雪崩, 万魔殿, 天空圣域, 湿地草原), plus two extra weather types (无常之雾, 王者的看破); the other 20 source-table types and four extra types remain design-only. Do not treat source-table text as proof of battle implementation.
- Some skills now use a first-use-started shared use window instead of plain cooldown or per-turn count. `WindowChargeSkill` in `src/wujiang/heroes/common.py` tracks a total use pool over N owner rounds, decremented on the owning hero's own turn start; leftover uses expire when the window ends, and UI labels should read from `window_*` public fields instead of only `max_uses_per_turn`.
- Multi-target wall reactions validate target count before resource prepayment. This matters for free multi-target wall variants such as `离子盾` and `量子盾`, and also avoids post-prepay validation failures on older wall skills.
- When a hostile queued action goes through a reaction window, the original queued payload now records whether the opponent reacted at all via `payload["enemy_reacted"]`. Hero-local traits can use that during final skill resolution without re-inspecting the chain window.
- Stealth hides logs and prevents direct hostile targeting, but point-cell skills can still hit if their cells overlap the stealthed unit. Stealthed units can still chain against enemy actions that affect them. Sandstorm suppresses all stealth statuses in its covered cells and removes only the stealth part, not non-stealth buffs from the same skill.
- Current battle UI direction: selected-unit state, controllable-unit strip, and chain info stay on sticky side rails, but actionable buttons are again stage-local and render around the currently selected unit. When a unit has many actions, lay them out as an adaptive clamped cluster around the piece instead of forcing them into the left rail. Replay controls use icon buttons with custom tooltips instead of native browser tooltips.
- Battlefield pan/zoom interaction rule: dragging must be startable directly from board cells or pieces, not just empty stage background. Wheel input inside `board-stage` should zoom around the pointer location rather than requiring the toolbar buttons.
- Multi-cell targeting UI rule: when an attack or skill only legally affects some occupied cells of a large unit, preview highlighting and clickability must stay restricted to those exact cells. Do not mark the whole unit footprint as targetable just because one occupied cell is valid.
- Battle inspection UI rule: even when the viewer cannot currently act, clicking any visible unit on the board must still switch the left rail to that unit's info/status view. Action availability and unit inspection are separate concerns.
- Current AI-UX note: live room state now exposes a staged `room.simulation.pending_action` payload for AI-controlled turns, reactions, respawns, and instant skills. `GameRoom` prepares the action first, then reveals selected cells/path one stage at a time, and only commits the battle action after the final confirmation delay. `static/app.js` should render `pending_action.visible_count` directly. There is no transient toast stack: battle narration lives only in the right-side log queue, and operation failures are written to the surface that owns the failed control.

## Current Hero Rules To Preserve

- Ellie:
  - Experiment uses the target's own round cadence for both the +2 all-stats buff and the death countdown. It does not tick down on every global turn end.
  - Crystal Ball lasts 4 of Ellie's own rounds, not 4 global hero turns.
- ElementHunter:
  - Complete Burn and Blizzard use remote rectangle selection that can truncate at board edges.
  - Plant Growth affects normal movement only, including flying units; extra cost triggers when the step starts inside the area.
  - Plant Growth lasts until ElementHunter's next own turn starts; it does not expire after two unrelated global turns.
  - EarthWalker costs 0 mana; clones cannot attack or use skills.
  - Great Fire Funeral fields do not stack overlapping area damage.
- UndeadKingLina:
  - Occupies `2*2`; attacks and range checks can originate from any occupied cell.
  - Rending is a one-battle skill that targets one cell by range, deals current attack damage, and pierces shields.
  - Wind Sand selects a remote `2*4` or `4*2` area and creates one-round Sandstorm weather if the damage area contains a unit.
  - Sandstorm: earth units/summons are immune to weather damage; flying non-earth units take `1/8`; other non-earth units take `1/16`; stealth cannot be used and active stealth is suppressed; evasion has no valid one-step move because its distance is reduced by 1.
  - Crazy Sand damages a straight line of 5 cells and teleports the caster to the 6th cell; invalid directions must not be selectable when the 6th anchor is out of bounds or occupied.
  - Lina basic attacks have half pierce, can attack twice, and lock the declared attack target until that target is destroyed.
  - Lina's attack lock tracks the effective attack recipient after mounted redirection; attacking a rider while mounted locks the mount, not the rider body.
  - Lina's destroy reward triggers once per turn only when her own attack or skill destroys a hero or a unit with current defense at least 4; it resets move/attack and gains the target's remaining current mana.
  - Lina prevents enemy healing within a `7*7` area around her and gains natural recovery in Sandstorm while not stealthed; natural mana recovery gains 1 mana.
- RockGod:
  - Occupies base `2*2`; body can become irregular through Rock Absorb and shrink through Rock Cannon. Remaining body must be orthogonally connected.
  - RockGod has a local Sandstorm aura covering the union of each occupied cell's surrounding `9*9`; local Sandstorm displays on board and only affects units inside those cells. RockGod sandstorm is ordinary same-name Sandstorm for stacking, so multiple RockGod auras and global Sandstorm do not deal duplicate weather damage.
  - Dragon Breath costs 2 mana, twice per turn, and selects a nearby edge-truncated `2*2` area that touches the caster orthogonally or diagonally.
  - Rock Absorb is once per turn; player chooses one stat, all units in RockGod's local Sandstorm except RockGod get that stat -1 for one round unless a shield blocks that unit's effect, and RockGod gains that stat by the successfully affected unit count even if a non-mana stat was already floored at 1. Mana absorption changes max mana and current mana. Shield reactions can block Rock Absorb per target.
  - Rock Absorb growth uses explicit selected cells, allows irregular shapes, requires orthogonal connectivity, and adds only as many legal cells as possible.
  - Rock Absorb footprint restoration attempts the base `2*2` at the current anchor. If a base cell is occupied or out of bounds, skip that cell and do not move RockGod to restore it.
  - Rock Cannon costs 0, has no use-count limit, selects one or more current body cells plus a direction, requires at least one body cell remain and the remainder stay orthogonally connected, rejects directions blocked by remaining own body, then each fired cell independently travels until hitting a unit or exiting at the boundary and resolves its own surrounding `3*3` damage for `3 + fired_cell_count` without pierce. Each impact damage is a separate chainable effect.
- DoomlightDragon:
  - Occupies `2*2`, has flying, and can heal above 1 hp.
  - Stone Wall is rule-identical to Light Wall.
  - Remote Dragon Breath is a range-based edge-truncated `2*2` damage skill, not a near-body pattern.
  - Doom Light is a once-per-battle remote `7*7` pure effect that pierces shields, lasts 4 rounds, blocks healing, and deals half-current-hp damage at each affected unit's own turn start.
  - Doom Light tick damage heals Doomlight Dragon by the same amount.
  - Units that attack Doomlight Dragon, damage it, or are damaged by it receive Doom Light, but Doom Light tick damage must not recursively refresh/reapply Doom Light.
  - Apocalypse is once per turn; the player explicitly chooses `n`, pays `n` hp where `n < current hp`, and then targets a remote edge-truncated `n*n` area for attack `+ n` shield-piercing damage. Example: current hp `1.25` still allows `n = 1`.
- Masamune:
  - Knight start rule is active: he enters battle already mounted on his own Motor Horse.
  - Arc Attack uses player-declared 8-direction facing. Orthogonal directions attack the outer 3-cell row; diagonal directions attack the corresponding 3-cell corner arc.
  - Mounted Free Leap is implemented as a visible 0-mana once-per-turn Leap action while mounted. It moves Masamune himself, not Motor Horse; if the landing cell is outside Motor Horse's footprint, Masamune dismounts.
- Jade:
  - Missile uses a first-use-started 2-round window counted on Jade's own turn starts, with expiring leftover uses.
  - Ion Shield is a free wall-like passive reaction with up to 2 casts per opposing hero turn; one cast may protect multiple threatened allies.
  - Quantum Shield is a free wall-like passive reaction with up to 3 casts per opposing hero turn in a usable own cycle; if used at all, the next own cycle is unavailable and the following cycle becomes usable again.
  - Laser uses remote edge-truncated `2*10` / `10*2` area selection.
  - Plasma Thruster is a straight flying move to the 5th cell, except boundary truncation allows the last in-bounds cell; occupied destinations remain invalid.
  - Plasma Thruster is a movement skill and is unavailable while Jade cannot move / cannot use movement skills.
  - Stance is a dynamic visible local field; it does not protect Jade, only blocks damage, and lasts from after Jade's current turn ends until Jade's next own turn starts.
  - Reactive Overclock checks damaging skills after enemy chain resolution; if any original enemy target took no damage, that skill gains +1 permanent future use from Jade's next own turn, once per turn per skill.
- N:
  - `磁力波` is the first formal instant skill: a range-based, edge-truncated `3*3` current-attack area skill paid with 2 mana points, usable once in each hero turn, including the opponent's current turn without a self-impact trigger.
  - `攻击魔力点+1` triggers on every basic-attack declaration, even if the later damage is blocked or the declared target cell ends up empty.
  - `每回合开始时决定攻击数=魔+1` snapshots once at own turn start using `floor(current_mana) + 1`; mid-turn mana changes do not alter that turn's attack cap.
  - `魔无上限` means both current mana and displayed mana cap are unbounded for N.
  - `魔大于0时不受到伤害，受到伤害时魔-1` applies per damage instance, blocks damage only, and still lets that same effect's non-damage follow-up apply.
- `分身` for N creates 3 clones in 3 separately chosen legal cells, then randomly swaps the real hero with one of those new clones. Allied views may distinguish the real body from clones, but enemy-facing public info must still mirror the real hero so mana, skill text, trait text, and clone markers do not reveal the real body. Its preview exposes legal single cells plus `required_cells=3`; do not enumerate every 3-cell combination, because large 6v6 boards make that combinatorial.
- BloodEater:
  - Excel `炎` is implemented as the existing `火` attribute.
  - Starts with 5 magic points, following the new default for future magic-point heroes.
  - Blood Art targets only self or allied heroes, must hit, grants attack +1 for 2 owner rounds, and the same BloodEater's copy cannot stack.
  - Blood Guard / 守*2 is once per turn, targets self or allied heroes, and follows the same same-caster non-stacking rule.
  - Blood Dance targets allied units; both target and BloodEater heal 1/4, gain 1 mana, and cannot move or use movement skills until that BloodEater's next own turn starts.
  - Sacrifice Ritual costs 4 magic points, revives any destroyed unit to a player-chosen legal adjacent cell, sets max/current hp to 1/4, and refills mana.
  - BloodEater gains 1 magic point, capped at 8, whenever any unit loses at least 1/4 hp from another unit's damage. At 8 magic points, skill damage is prevented but non-damage effects and healing still apply.
- 天蟹 / `excel_r070`:
  - `heaven_punishment` / 天罚 can only target enemy units that currently expose at least one active skill to seal. Units without active skills, such as clones or exhausted no-skill summons, must be excluded from preview and AI payloads.
- Li:
  - Chain Pull / 链条 is the same as common Lock Chain: pay 0.5 mana; once per turn; choose a straight front 5-cell line; the first hit unit is pulled straight to a legal cell around Li.
  - Red Heat / 红热 is a start-phase toggle: open gives attack +2 and speed +3, and Li loses half current hp at Li's own turn end; using it again in a later start phase closes it.
  - Essence / 精华 gives exactly 2 extra shield-piercing basic attacks this turn; only leftover extra charges convert to current mana at turn end.
  - Foresight / 见切 is a passive once per opposing hero turn: block one enemy basic attack, then Li's next own turn gains attack count +1 and speed +1.
  - Stillness / 定 is a once-per-battle ultimate: heal Li by 1/2, end Red Heat, then for 4 owner rounds Li cannot act, gains defense +2, magic immunity, and natural healing at own turn start.
  - Li may split normal movement any number of times but total normal movement distance is still capped by current speed; Li also ignores enemy no-displacement and speed-reduction effects.
- Chanter:
  - The three cards are permanent non-occupying field markers until Chanter leaves the battlefield. Their normal affected area is the hit center cell plus its surrounding `3*3`.
  - Paralysis Card dynamically prevents enemy units in that area from using skills; Poison Card deals attack-power-2 piercing field damage at affected enemies' own turn end; Drain Card drains 1 mana to Chanter at affected enemies' own turn end. Card effects are piercing.
  - Light Wall is exactly the common Light Wall because no extra text is written.
  - Card Transposition is passive: when Chanter is affected by an enemy action, Chanter may swap with one own card not currently under Chanter. The original action still resolves on the originally declared cells.
  - Magic Claw targets one own card but affects only the surrounding cells, excluding the card's center cell; enemies there cannot move or use movement skills until Chanter's next own turn starts. The card remains.
  - Form Shift is a permanent once-per-battle transformation to attack 4 / defense 5 / speed 4 / range 1; it disables future use of the three card-placement skills only, while existing cards remain active and Light Wall, Card Transposition, and Magic Claw remain usable.
- ErasureApostle:
  - `￥/¥` skills are ultimates: Extra Stealth is once per battle and uses the same stealth state; Erasure is once per battle but resets when ErasureApostle destroys a hero with its own attack or skill.
  - Premature Burial is a once-per-turn range-5 unit target that must hit, pierces shields, and places one stackable Erasure Counter from this ErasureApostle.
  - Erasure removes only this ErasureApostle's own counters from all units, then each removed counter causes direct `1/4` hp loss with shield pierce, not attack/defense formula damage.
  - Descent Moment costs 1 mana, once per turn, targets an enemy with any erasure counter, lets the player choose any legal adjacent landing cell, then grants 2 extra basic attacks locked to that target only; the original normal basic attack may still target others.
  - Shadow Counter is a speed-2 passive costing 0.5 mana with no use-count limit: when self is affected by an enemy attack/skill, normal-move exactly 2 cells without passing through units, then place non-piercing own counters on enemy units in the original-position surrounding `5*5` excluding the origin cell. Shields and magic immunity can block the counter placement.
  - Destroy reward triggers only from this ErasureApostle's own attack/skill kills, gains the destroyed unit's remaining current mana, and resets Erasure only when the killed target is a hero.
- DragonRider:
  - Excel `炎` is implemented as `火`. DragonRider is a Knight and starts battle mounted on its own `2*2` Dragon summon.
  - Dragon summon stats are attack 3 / defense 5 / speed 5 / range 4 / mana 0, flying, rideable, and capped to one own Dragon. If destroyed, the rider must wait through one own turn before resummoning.
  - Dragon's basic attack is an area-declared remote `3*3` attack. Its damage is not piercing, but the hit follow-up speed -1 / defense -1 is piercing and lasts until DragonRider's next own turn starts.
  - Dragon reduces formula skill damage by 1 to a floor of 1 and caps multi-cell area bonus to one hit cell; fixed/raw hp loss is not reduced.
  - Dragon Slash is once per turn: front straight 5 cells, damage value 5, non-piercing damage, but piercing chain-pull follow-up on the first hit unit; that unit gets speed -2 until DragonRider's next own turn starts.
  - Smoke Spray costs 1 mana, once per turn, remote `3*6` / `6*3`, no damage, affects both sides, persists until DragonRider's next own turn starts, and blocks attacks plus active skills for units inside or later entering the area.
  - Trait: at DragonRider's own turn start, gain 1 current mana per fielded allied non-summon non-clone Mage hero.
- SoulWraith:
  - Stats are level 1 Swordsman / Dark / Spirit, attack 4 / defense 0.5 / speed 5 / range 1 / mana 2; defense floor for this hero is 0.5.
  - Uses common Pierce. Physical Immunity blocks enemy basic-attack damage and attack follow-up effects, but not skills.
  - Pass-through movement lets normal movement paths cross units while final placement remains non-overlapping; Flying also applies.
  - Arc Attack is a declared 8-direction basic area attack and now resolves against all enemy units in the three declared arc cells.
  - Attack Mana Drain drains up to 1 current mana from each enemy that actually takes SoulWraith basic-attack damage and gives the same amount to SoulWraith.
  - If no enemy hero body is in SoulWraith's surrounding 8 cells, SoulWraith has magic immunity by the durable definition. Enemy summons/clones do not disable it.
  - If SoulWraith's basic attack deals no damage because an enemy or enemy teammate used a reaction skill, SoulWraith gains a stacking status: attack +1, speed +1, and normal-move actions per turn +1. It persists across turns until a SoulWraith basic attack deals damage; Pierce neither triggers nor clears it.
- AI audit batch 015 (`excel_r093`, `excel_r094`, `excel_r113`):
  - Kaiser AI recognizes both Large Pierce and Kaiser Fist. Kaiser Fist uses attack +1 for damage estimation and values the up-to-2 mana refund when final damage is zero.
  - Noise AI separately scores Interference's clone destruction/summon takeover and Noise Wave's piercing no-damage control, including allied collateral. Equivalent area payloads that hit the same unit set are collapsed only during AI search.
  - Ernest's Purify and Sacred Duel expose current-range/current-line direct enemy previews. Sacred Duel is shield-piercing; both skills use dedicated non-fictional AI scoring.
  - A prepaid multi-target wall whose declared target leaves protection range during a faster reaction now logs no effect rather than throwing during reverse-order chain resolution.
- AI audit batch 016 (`excel_r118`, `excel_r123`, `excel_r127`):
  - Zerozaki AI scores all units crossed by Zero Dash and the resulting mana gain instead of treating the exact-8 move as ordinary repositioning.
  - Fuma AI scores Pursuit's four damage cells plus landing, persistent Trap control, and Shuriken line damage. Equivalent Shuriken area payloads are collapsed by hit signature only during AI search.
  - Fantasy Bird AI builds compound target-and-destination payloads for Fantasy and Rainbow Mirror, and values Friendly Mirror against enemies whose current attack can trigger it.
  - Skills that cannot be evaded must expose `cannot_evade` on the queued action before the chain window opens. Passive Evasion rejects those queued actions; setting the flag only on the later damage context is too late.
- AI audit batch 017 (`excel_r136`, `excel_r137`, `excel_r139`):
  - Oboro AI builds target-plus-exact-5-cell-landing payloads for True Blade Air Slash, scores its defense-based piercing damage and mana gain, and does not spend Meditation's cooldown for less than 0.5 recoverable mana.
  - Undead Boy's Devour exposes current-range/current-line enemy targets. AI evaluates its raw half-current-hp damage and self-heal instead of treating it as a generic target skill.
  - Sola AI evaluates Illumination Light at attack power 4, including dark-only shield pierce, and omits it when no enemy hero body is inside the aura.
  - Fusion's Nuclear Rush basic attack requires its mandatory fifth-cell movement. While Fusion cannot move, the direction preview is empty and AI cannot queue an attack that would fail after damage resolution.
- AI audit batch 018 (`excel_r158`, `excel_r166`, `excel_r187`):
  - Magic Warrior AI treats Hell Slash as a damaging area ultimate and scores Martial God Seal from its real stat, healing, and mana value without recasting an active seal.
  - Electric Man AI scores Electric Wind as no-damage skill denial plus speed control, skips already affected enemies, and penalizes allied collateral.
  - Demon Leader AI opens Pandemonium only when the weather is absent and combat targets exist. Creating it immediately synchronizes every fielded Demon Leader's weather speed trait.
  - Generic `DashMoveSkill` availability and preview reject movement while `cannot_move` is active, keeping frontend actions, engine legality, AI payload generation, and match-audit diagnostics consistent.
- AI audit batch 019 (`excel_r188`, `excel_r326`, `excel_r337`):
  - Light Road Guardian exposes the source skill name `天使的气息` separately from its created weather `天空的圣域`. AI opens the weather while absent and recognizes `vitality_blast` as damaging line payloads.
  - Florenza's `vain_giant_shadow` exposes current-range/current-line direct unit previews and marks `cannot_evade` before the chain window. AI prioritizes enemy attack denial, avoids already disabled targets, and does not mistake the target's attack bonus for pure hostile value.
  - Tina's `wetland_grassland` has state-aware once-per-battle weather scoring; common Heal keeps missing-health scoring.

## Frontend Coupling

- BP 默认战场为横向 `16×11`。`multiplayer.GameRoom` 保存默认与自定义尺寸，`heroes.registry.bp_spawn_positions` 在默认盘面按同顺位、同行的左右槽位放置：蓝队槽位横坐标恒为红队对应槽位 `+11`，两者之间空 10 格。3人用单列，5人用两侧错列四位加中位，双方位置集合旋转对称；纯一格阵容的同队格距至少 3。多格单位优先留空并在无法满足时保底合法不重叠，自定义尺寸沿用既有入场算法。改动时一起检查房间尺寸公开状态、开战传参和前端设置。
- 自走棋装备资料由 `autochess.public().equipment_catalog` 提供。`static/tactical/autochess-ui.js` 的背包按钮与武将已装备标签仅常显名称，鼠标悬停或键盘聚焦触发同一完整属性/效果浮层；浮层在 `document.body` 上以避免侧栏裁剪，长文本换行、限定视口并可滚动。装备抽取按钮只表示随机抽取，抽取前没有可预览的具体装备。

- If backend target selection uses `occupied_cells`, update `static/app.js` previews/click helpers too.
- `unitOccupiedCells(unit)` is the frontend fallback-compatible helper for multi-cell units.
- Multi-cell units render as one footprint-spanning board piece, not one piece per occupied cell. Irregular pieces use `occupied_cells` to fill only actual body cells within the bounding box. Keep board cells explicitly grid-positioned so overlay pieces do not disturb grid auto-placement.
- Keep action wheel buttons and board alerts at a higher CSS stacking layer than footprint-spanning board pieces; large units can otherwise visually cover skill buttons even when pointer events are disabled.
- Action wheel and board-alert overlays are stage-level absolute layers, so any zoom, board-stage scroll, or window resize path must reschedule overlay positioning after layout settles.
- Action wheel placement must also clamp back inside the visible `board-stage` bounds when the selected unit is near an edge; do not let top or side buttons render outside the scroll viewport.
- During target selection, the action wheel must hide so its floating buttons cannot cover clickable board cells; use board highlights plus cancel/complete controls for the targeting step.
- During target or respawn selection, board-stage drag/pointer capture must be disabled so single clicks continue to land on the intended `.cell`.
- Battle VFX are also stage-level overlays driven from backend `visual_events`. Engine changes that alter attack/skill/effect timing or defensive cancellation should keep `visual_events` synchronized, and frontend changes must preserve VFX positioning after board rerenders.
- Battle feedback must remain redundant: color/animation/audio are optional enhancements, while icon+text messages, board-cell accessible names, and the assertive live region carry the same critical attack, defense, chain, death, and outcome information. Do not create an audio context before explicit player opt-in, and do not replay live feedback while scrubbing historical replay steps.
- Move-path previews should show a distinct final-footprint highlight and translate clicks on any final occupied footprint cell back to the backend anchor payload.
- Frontend move blocking must mirror `battle.can_place_unit(...)`: test the whole footprint, allow overlaps only for stealth exceptions, and reject out-of-bounds footprints.
- Pattern-cell skills must expose target cells through skill previews and `get_target_cells_for_payload` so chain windows show the affected area.
- Custom frontend selection modes currently include `stat_cells` for Rock Absorb, `body_direction` for Rock Cannon, and `choice_pattern` for skills that need both an explicit option choice and a shaped area selection such as Apocalypse. Backend remains authoritative for legality. Rock Cannon body-cell selection must visibly highlight selectable and selected occupied body cells, including large/irregular footprint cells.
- Multiplayer room identity is stored in both `localStorage` and `sessionStorage` and auto-loaded from `?room=...`; if a stored token no longer maps to a viewer seat, clear it so the user is not stuck as a spectator with a stale token.
- Current room / online implementation remains two-seat, but classic mode now needs per-seat multi-hero rosters and hero-counter lobby UI. Preserve the per-hero interpretation of `回合`, `己方回合`, and `轮`.
- In running or full rooms, `/api/rooms/join` may reclaim an occupied seat by exact normalized player name and returns the original token/player id. Open lobbies still let a same-name player claim an open second seat. The frontend exposes a recovery button when the current nickname matches an occupied seat but the viewer has no token.
- `/favicon.ico` intentionally returns `204 No Content`; the icon is not gameplay-critical and should not create distracting 404 console noise.
- Postgame summaries are server-authored from actual HP loss/recovery, not requested damage/heal values. Attribute summons, clones, and mounts to their controlling root hero; keep shield breaks separate from damage. The finished-room payload must expose the explicit win reason, winner-side MVP score breakdown, per-team/per-hero totals, and up to four replay-linked key turns. Frontend result cards consume this payload and key-turn actions jump through the existing replay loader instead of reconstructing combat events client-side.
- Finished custom-room rematches are host-initiated and preserve mode, seat/team/controller layout, rosters, random quotas, and AI difficulty. They clear the old battle/replay and all human readiness, then require fresh confirmations before the next battle. Quick AI remains the immediate same-roster new-room path.
- The account panel on the room-home screen is mandatory for creating or joining rooms. Login/register syncs the account username into the existing nickname flow, but current room ownership still uses the existing per-room token until strategy saves and account permissions are implemented.
- Strategy mode UI direction: prioritize a game-like map command screen over webpage/card-grid layout. The strategy home now renders a browser-validated war-room shell with a map-first city node stage, HUD resource strip, a "next step" monthly guidance panel, map legend, and a selected-city command panel grouped into governance, recruitment, rebellion, and attack actions. Pending story events appear before routine briefings, expose readable choice consequences and unresolved future threads, and can replace an already queued choice even when the faction budget is otherwise full. The draft home has a mode-gateway menu that puts "英灵城邦战役" and "武将对战房间" before lower-priority forms, routes unauthenticated mode clicks into the account gate, and keeps the long reference hero roster hidden on that home screen. Creating, joining, locking, or entering a strategy campaign must focus the war room instead of leaving the player in lower records; strategy guide buttons include map/command shortcuts, and mobile city-map clicks should jump to the command panel so feedback and actions are immediately visible. The war room includes a top-level state banner for lobby/resume blockers and host lock action, compact map/command/dossier tabs for mobile reachability, city map nodes show queued-action badges, selected-city command cards summarize already planned orders and city order capacity, and city/order interactions raise transient toasts for immediate feedback. Strategy panel select/input focus must suppress poll-driven strategy re-render so dropdowns do not collapse while players choose options. Map city nodes must preserve their centered transform on hover and have stable hit targets; route lists live behind a compact "路线情报" drawer instead of occupying the map surface, and route/dossier drawers use explicit frontend state so polling rerenders do not make them appear unclickable. Recruitment command sections include a local search filter before the hero select so large hero pools stay usable. Mobile map/command/dossier targets need scroll-margin under sticky tabs so the tabs do not cover the target panel. Secondary campaign/member/log/hero/tech details are collapsed into the "战报卷宗" surface by default, and that surface should use internal dossier tabs such as members/orders/objectives/battles/heroes/tech/events instead of exposing a single long webpage-like list. Web strategy create/join/lock/enter paths should mark the current account online so the resume gate does not show the acting player as absent.
- Strategy hero identity is actor-first: each player/AI controls one persistent strategic hero, which may be a lord, remain roaming, found a faction at its current city, or request allegiance to another lord. A roaming player gets a dedicated map workspace and no faction/city commands. Additional faction heroes are obtained through the confirmed city ritual-site random summon rule: the ritual pays 30 city ether and one command point, requires free non-lord office capacity, and binds the summoned hero to that city. Acceptance/allegiance or ritual summoning never grants an office automatically; the lord must appoint an unassigned serving hero as grand general, general, or governor. A campaign-founded faction starts with only its founding lord, and office migration must never synthesize free heroes.

- 2026-10-25 OOP回归入口续批：`tests/test_behavior.py::frontend_script`恢复ES模块私有词法作用域，前端渲染专项收敛到32项仅1失败；余下城主注册士兵入口缺失待产品归因。`static/tactical/room-lobby.js::confirmRoomSetup`现在只为标准/随机模式提交改变的席位数，BP/自走棋保持固定席位；定向场景、前端模块/全局/浏览器启动及战斗+AI预测493项通过。完整unittest仍未绿，见`docs/武将OOP重构计划.md`。

## Testing Policy

- BP 特殊模式的阵亡判定必须覆盖伤害结算与非伤害途径：统一阵亡清理在移除单位前通知房间规则，处理骤死、软柿子替死与擂台接替；替死者要标记以防递归替死。当前生命与当前魔量的直接增加必须走与回复 API 一致的 BP 禁止回复/回复反噬判定。
- BP“同情弱者”的直接伤害限制优先读取 `Skill.directly_damages_on_use()` 对象能力；通过后续动作伤害的技能（如 R324 四种飞鸦阵）必须在本技能类明确声明，不能只依赖源码关键词。法师模式的隐身例外只跳过模式满魔，不取消角色正常回合回魔。
- BP“铁链”跟随应沿开场同队行动顺序继续传递，且不得绕回推动本次最初移动的单位；跟随移动不消耗行动资源。
- 回归夹具若直接把敌城、军队或围城传送进战局，读取按阵营过滤的 HTTP 公开状态前须显式建立玩家已知城市；不要因匿名/被遮蔽响应缺字段就改生产视野。AI 换位场景需让回避带来明确净收益，不能仅把致命伤害转给另一名友军就断言 AI 必须换位。
- 并行完整 AI 计划的 `_army_anchor_cache` 可以合法不存在；工作线程与父进程必须区分无字段和存在但无效的缓存，仍用完整提交快照、别名、随机数与两路编号校验。战略 AI 测试如需断言敌城内部政策，读取存档世界；公开 HTTP 响应遵守玩家视野。

- Add focused tests for every durable gameplay rule change.
- After every gameplay, room-flow, or player-visible UI change, add or update at least one behavior scenario that exercises the changed feature from the player's point of view.
- Prefer `tests/test_behavior.py` for scenario-style coverage that crosses room APIs, turn flow, chain windows, targeting, or frontend-facing state payloads.
- Use direct `DamageContext` tests only for engine-level behavior; prefer `battle.perform_action(...)` for player-facing skill behavior.
- Run targeted tests for the changed rule first.
- Run the relevant behavior scenarios after targeted tests.
- Run `python -m unittest discover -s tests` before final response when feasible.
- `git diff --check` may show LF/CRLF warnings on this Windows repo; treat actual whitespace errors separately from those warnings.
